### Description

The City of Lake Wales Recreation proposes to host Christmas Cinema with Santa at Oakley Park on December 19, 2026, from 4:00 p.m. to 8:00 p.m. The event description is a Christmas movie in the park with photo opportunities with Santa, vendors and other holiday activities. Setup and takedown are listed for the same day from 2:00 p.m. to 9:00 p.m.

The City’s application identifies portable toilets, inflatables/bounce houses, food distribution and sales, on-site cooking, use of electrical outlets and water spigots, and handicap parking and restrooms. These components indicate potential rental or event-support needs for a City-hosted constituent event, although the source does not explicitly request an outside vendor or identify which equipment the City already owns. Fiscal impact is unknown, and no supplier is selected.

## Attachments (1). Later amendments replace earlier text.

### 11172026Plus.pdf (posted 2026-11-16)

Summary: The official agenda schedules the Lake Wales City Commission’s regular meeting for November 17, 2026, at 6:00 p.m. in the Municipal Administration Building Commission Chambers at 201 W. Central Avenue, Lake Wales, Florida.

The meeting includes standard opening procedures, public comments, proclamations, commission and mayor comments, and opportunities for residents, taxpayers, business owners, and utility customers to address the Commission for up to five minutes.

The consent agenda includes:

- Approval of prior meeting minutes.
- An amendment to the Polk County interlocal agreement for the Ridge Scenic Highway Connector Trail to fund construction, engineering, inspection, and post-design services not covered by the FDOT grant.
- A proposed $40,000 payment for Orange Blossom Revue infrastructure.
- Furniture procurement for the new police annex.
- Permits for the February 5–6, 2027, Lake Wales Mardi Gras celebration, including the parade, fireworks, temporary street closures, and alcohol use.
- A December 19, 2026, Christmas Cinema with Santa event at Oakley Park requiring closure of Cypress Garden Lane and part of Crystal Avenue from 2:00 to 8:00 p.m.
- Revised Verizon Wireless water-tower lease terms for Contract No. 50502, including a $4,000 monthly payment beginning July 1, 2027; 15% increases every five years; and five additional five-year renewal terms.
- A $139,264.92 award to Professional Piping Services to clean the Eagle Ridge Mall Area and Southside wastewater force mains.
- Utility easement acquisition expenditures not to exceed $211,000 for the Hunt Brothers Road Utility Improvements Project.

Old and new business centers on budget, land-use, zoning, development, and annexation actions.

Ordinance 2026-40 receives its second reading and public hearing for the first FY 2025–26 budget amendment.

Ordinances 2026-35 and 2026-36 propose changing approximately 0.5 acres from Medium Density Residential/R-3 residential to Residential Office/PF Professional for a MidFlorida parking-related use, while Ordinance 2026-41 would amend portions of Chapter 23 of the Land Development Regulations.

The Commission will also conduct first readings and public hearings on a coordinated Oakwood Ridge proposal involving annexation of approximately 393 acres, future land-use and zoning map changes, commercial designations, and a preliminary subdivision plat for 558 single-family homes and 5.195 acres of neighborhood commercial development.

Supporting materials include an impact analysis, FDOT review concerning SR 60 and Capps Road, noticed-property information, a tree-preservation statement, a master site plan, and a project narrative.

Public participation from residents, taxpayers, business owners, and utility
customers is encouraged. The City Commission welcomes information that would
assist them in considering agenda items, City business, and otherwise fulfilling
the City’s mission to make Lake Wales “a bit better or more beautiful” for its
citizens.
Anyone wishing to speak should give their name, state if they are a resident,
taxpayer, business owner, or utility customer and they will have 5 minutes.
Courtesy and respect is the hallmark. Speakers are expected to address the
Commission and not audience members, and not engage in back and forth
discussions that can deteriorate into argument, debate and accusations.
Questions about subject matter are best directed to city staff during normal
business hours

MEMORANDUM
DATE:	November 6, 2026
TO:	City Commission
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
FROM:	Stephanie-Grace Lutton
SUBJECT:  Interlocal Agreement for Ridge Scenic Highway Connector Trail
SYNOPSIS: Approval of an Amendment to the Interlocal Agreement with Polk County to
cover Construction, Engineering, and Inspection (CEI) and post-design service costs not
funded by the FDOT grant for the Ridge Scenic Highway (RSH) Connector Trail.
RECOMMENDATION
Staff recommends that the City Commission approve the Amendment to the Interlocal
Agreement with Polk County to provide for the payment of construction-phase
engineering and inspection costs not covered by the grant, and authorize the Mayor to
execute the amendment.
BACKGROUND
The Ridge Scenic Highway (RSH) Connector Trail is nearly ready to advertise for bids.
This is a Florida Department of Transportation (FDOT) project, with Polk County acting
as the Local Agency Program (LAP) entity on behalf of the City.
In January 2021, the City submitted a TPO/FDOT Priority Transportation Project
Application for the RSH Connector Trail. The City was awarded the grant, and staff has
been working with FDOT and the County to finalize the design and submit the bid
package. However, when the original grant application was submitted, funding for
Construction, Engineering, and Inspection (CEI) and post-design services was not
included in the request; therefore, the grant did not cover all required services during
the construction phase.
The CEI contractor will be AIM, one of FDOT’s continuing service contract vendors, and
post-design services will be performed by KCA, the firm that designed the trail. FDOT is

currently researching whether any additional grant funding is available to offset these
costs.
FISCAL IMPACT
CEI – estimated at $153,572.11; Post Design Services - $21,261. There may be
additional Grant Funding available. These funds will be paid by the CRA.
ATTACHMENT  – see next page
Amendment to Interlocal with Polk County for LAP Certified Project RSH Connector Trail
Interlocal Agreement with BOCC
Projected CEI Cost Estimate
FDOT draft contract with KCA

INTERLOCAL AGREEMENT  BETWEEN  POLK COUNTY AND
CITY OF LAKE WALES FOR THE LOCAL AGENCY PROGRAM GRANT
RELATED  TO RIDGE SCENIC HIGHWAY CONNECTOR TRAIL
AMENDMENT  #1
This Amendment #1 is hereby entered into as of the Amendment Effective Date,
defined in Section 5 below, by and between Polk County, a political subdivision of the
State of Florida (the "POLK COUNTY"), and the City of Lake Wales, a Florida municipal
corporation (the "CITY"). POLK COUNTY and CITY shall collectively be referred to as
the "Parties."
WITNESSETH:
WHEREAS, the Parties entered into that certain lnterlocal Agreement for the Local
Agency Program (LAP) Grant from the State of Florida for the Ridge Scenic Highway
Connector Trail project (the "AGREEMENT"); and
WHEREAS, the Parties have agreed to revise the Agreement to specify the CITY's
responsibilities for cost overages for the PROJECT, as further set forth below.
NOW, THEREFORE, the Parties hereby agree to the following:
1.  The recitals stated above are true and correct and are fully incorporated herein.
2.  Section 2.2, The City's Responsibilities, is hereby deleted in its entirety and
replaced with the following:
The City's Responsibilities. The CITY shall be responsible for any and all costs
associated with the PROJECT for construction, and associated CEI and Post
Design Services costs in excess of what funding is provided in the LAP
Agreement. The CITY shall appoint a project manager to coordinate with the
County. The CITY shall pay POLK COUNTY within 60 days of receipt of an
invoice.
3.  The AGREEMENT, as amended by this Amendment #1, continues in full force
and effect.
4.  Except as expressly provided in this Amendment #1, no other terms, conditions,
provisions, or obligations of the AGREEMENT have been amended or altered
and, thus, shall remain in full force and effect.
5.  This Amendment #1 shall become effective upon the date in which the last Party
executes this Amendment #1 (the "Amendment Effective Date").

IN WITNESS  WHEREOF,	the Parties hereto, through their duly authorized
representatives have made and executed this Amendment #1 on the date indicated
below.
POLK COUNTY, a Political Subdivision of the
State of Florida

ANINTERLOCALAGREEMENT
BETWEEN	THE  CITY OF LAKE  WALES,  FLORIDA
AND  POLK  COUNTY,  FLORIDA	FOR THE  RIDGE  SCENIC HIGHWAY
CONNECTOR	TRAIL
This lnterlocal Agreement (the "AGREEMENT") is made and entered into by
and between the CITY OF LAKE WALES, a municipal corporation of the State of Florida,
whose address is 201 Central Avenue W., Lake Wales, Florida 33859, (the "CITY") and
POLK COUNTY, a political subdivision of the State of Florida, whose address is 330 West
Church Street, Bartow, Florida 33830 ("POLK COUNTY") (the CITY and POLK COUNTY
are referred to herein individually as a "Party" and collectively as the "Parties").
WITNESSETH
WHEREAS,  Section 163.01, Florida Statutes, Florida lnterlocal Cooperation
Act, authorizes local governments to enter into lnterlocal Agreements to ensure the most
efficient use of their respective powers and to exercise, jointly, any power, privilege, or
authority which such agencies share in common and which each might exercise
separately; and
WHEREAS, the CITY has received a Local Agency Program (LAP) Grant from
the State of Florida for the Ridge Scenic Highway Connector Trail project ("PROJECT");
and
WHEREAS, the CITY is not LAP Certified to manage this PROJECT; and
WHEREAS, the COUNTY is LAP Certified to manage this PROJECT; and
WHEREAS,  POLK COUNTY  has agreed to manage this PROJECT for the
CITY; and
WHEREAS,  POLK COUNTY  and the CITY have agreed to enter into this
AGREEMENT  as set forth to establish the respective rights of the CITY and POLK
COUNTY in accordance with the terms and conditions of this AGREEMENT.
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises contained herein
and other valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby
acknowledged the Parties agree as follows:
1 of 7

ARTICLE I
RECITALS AND DEFINITIONS
1 .1 Recitals. The foregoing recitals are incorporated herein by reference as if recited in full
and serve as a factual stipulation upon which the Parties agree is the basis for this
Agreement.
1 .2 Definitions. The capitalized terms contained herein shall be defined as set forth in the
above recitals or as specified in the following paragraphs.
ARTICLE II
AGREEMENT
2.1 The County's Responsibilities. POLK COUNTY agrees to manage the PROJECT
through construction as required by the LAP Agreement. This will include the bidding
out of the RFP for construction. The COUNTY shall have no financial responsibility for
this PROJECT.
2.2 The City's Responsjbjlities. The CITY shall be responsible for any and all costs
associated with this project that are in excess of what funding is provided in the LAP
Agreement. The CITY shall appoint a project manager to coordinate with the County.
The CITY will use FDOT's continuing services contract to design the PROJECT.
ARTICLE Ill
MISCELLANEOUS  PROVISIONS
3.1  Notjce, Whenever a Party desires to give notice to any other Party, it must be given by
written notice delivered: (i) via registered or certified United States mail, postage
prepaid with return receipt requested or (ii) via nationally recognized overnight delivery
service, and addressed to the Party for whom it is intended at the place last specified
by each Party. Notices hereunder may also be delivered by counsel. The place for
giving notice shall remain such until it is changed by written notice delivered in
compliance with the provisions of this Section. For the present, the Parties designate
the following as the respective places for giving of notice:

3.2	Severability. If any section, phrase, sentence or portion of this Agreement is,
for any reason, held to be invalid by any court of competent jurisdiction, such portion shall
be deemed as a separate, distinct, and independent provision, and such holding shall not
affect the validity of the remaining portions hereof.
3.3	Effective Date. The Effective Date shall be date in which the last Party
executes this Agreement.
3.4	Days. The term days in this Agreement shall mean calendar days unless
otherwise so noted. If a date for performance falls on a Saturday, Sunday or legal State
of Florida or federal holiday, the date for performance shall be extended until the next
calendar day that is not a Saturday, Sunday or federal holiday.
3.5	Default and Remedy. If any Party materially defaults in its obligations under
this Agreement and fails to cure the same within thirty (30) days after the date the Party
receives written notice of the default from a non-defaulting Party, then the non-defaulting
Party shall have the right to (i) immediately terminate this Agreement by delivering written
notice to the materially defaulting Party (after expiration of the foregoing cure period), and
(ii) pursue any and all remedies available in law, equity, and under this Agreement (after
expiration of the foregoing cure period).
3.6	Limitation of Liability. IN NO EVENT, SHALL ANY PARTY BE LIABLE TO
ANY OTHER  PARTY FOR INDIRECT, INCIDENTAL, CONSEQUENTIAL, SPECIAL,
3 of 7

EXEMPLARY, OR PUNITIVE DAMAGES  OF ANY KIND OR NATURE, INCLUDING
LOSS OF PROFIT, WHETHER  FORESEEABLE  OR NOT, ARISING OUT OF OR
RESULTING FROM THE NONPERFORMANCE  OR BREACH OF THIS CONTRACT BY
THE DEFAULTING PARTY  WHETHER  BASED  IN CONTRACT, COMMON	LAW,
WARRANTY,  TORT,  STRICT  LIABILITY, CONTRIBUTION, INDEMNITY OR
OTHERWISE.
3. 7	Waiver. A waiver by any Party of any breach of this Agreement shall not be
binding upon the waiving Party unless such waiver is in writing. In the event of a written
waiver, such a waiver shall not affect the waiving Party's rights with respect to any other
·or further breach of this Agreement. The making or acceptance of a payment by any Party
with the knowledge of any other Party's existing default or breach of the Agreement shall
not waive such default or breach, or any subsequent default or breach of this Agreement,
and shall not be construed as doing so.
3.8	Attorneys' Fees and Costs. Each Party shall be responsible for its own legal
and attorneys' fees, costs and expenses incurred in connection with any dispute or any
litigation arising out of, or relating to this Agreement, including attorneys' fees, costs, and
expenses incurred for any appellate or bankruptcy proceedings.
3.9	Modification or Extension. This Agreement may only be modified or extended
in time by a written amendment properly executed by the Parties. No oral modifications
will be effective or binding.
3.10	Integration. This Agreement sets-forth the entire agreement between the
Parties with respect to its subject matter and that there are no promises or understandings
other than those stated herein. This Agreement supersedes all prior agreements,
contracts, proposals, representations, negotiations, letters, or other communications
between the Parties pertaining to the PROJECT, whether written or oral.
3.11	Counter parts. This Agreement may be executed in multiple counterparts each
of which shall be an original, but which collectively shall form a single agreement.
3.12	Governing Law and Venue. This Agreement and the rights and obligations of
the Parties hereunder shall be interpreted, governed by, construed under, and enforced
in accordance with the applicable laws of the State of Florida, and the ordinances, rules
and regulations of Polk County including, but not limited to the Polk County
Comprehensive Plan, Land Development Code and Utility Code, and any amendments
thereto in effect as of the Effective Date of this Agreement. The Parties hereby consent
to the sole and exclusive jurisdiction and venue for any action relating to the construction,
interpretation, or enforcement of this Agreement to be in or for the Tenth Judicial Circuit,
in Polk County, Florida.

1

PROFESSIONAL	ENGINEERING  CONSULTING	SERVICES
This Exhibit forms an integral part of the agreement between Polk County (hereinafter referred to as the
COUNTY) and (Kisinger Campo & Associates, Corp.), (hereinafter referred to as the CONSULTANT)
relative to the transportation facility described as follows:
I.	PROJECT  TITLE
Professional Engineering Services (Post-Design Services) for RSH Connector Trail Project (LAP).
II.	PURPOSE  AND BACKGROUND
The overall purpose and objective of this document is to describe the scope of work and responsibilities of
the CONSULTANT for Post-Design Services to assist the COUNTY with bid documents during the
construction of 8-foot wide concrete trail, lighting and crosswalks; address requests for information (RFIs)
from the contractor and COUNTY technical staff; and review shop drawings to facilitate a satisfactorily
complete construction.
These services are not intended to address any CONSULTANT errors and/or omissions that may
occur. The hours required to complete these tasks may be increased or decreased by the COUNTY
based on actual project requirements.
The following items are representative, but not limited to, services related to Construction
Assistance to be provided to the CONSULTANT. During the Construction Phase, it is understood a
COUNTY DESIGNEE will administer the construction contract, perform records management, and
carry out resident engineering and inspection services.
The CONSULTANT will furnish the following services:
• Attend the pre-bid conference and assist the COUNTY DESIGNEE as needed.
• Construction Meetings: Assist the COUNTY DESIGNEE as needed during the
construction of the sidewalks, driveways, and crosswalks.
• Plan Interpretations and Requests for Additional Information (RAIs):
Provide written interpretations to the COUNTY DESIGNEE of the Contract
Documents (Plans, Specifications, and Bid Package) and respond to questions
concerning unforeseen conditions affecting the project’s design and construction.
The CONSULTANT will not direct or infer changes to the construction contract.
Changes will be submitted through the COUNTY’S change order procedure.
• Review the Contractor’s Shop Drawings
• Site Visits: Respond to problems encountered during construction and conduct field
investigations as requested by the COUNTY DESIGNEE. Based on information
obtained during visits to the construction site, the CONSULTANT will assist the
COUNTY in providing potential solutions to the problems encountered including
any plan modifications that may be required.
• Field Changes and Plan Revisions: From time to time during construction the
CONSULTANT may be requested by the COUNTY DESIGNEE to review
contractor proposed field changes or respond with a recommended solution to
remedy particular field situations not covered by the plans and specifications. The
CONSULTANT will revise the plans to provide for changes that the COUNTY
deems necessary after final plan acceptance.
2

• Meetings and Coordination: During the construction phase, the CONSULTANT will
attend meetings held between the contractor and COUNTY DESIGNEE as requested.
Attendance and coordination with other COUNTY departments, adjacent property owners,
and utility representatives may also be required.
III. EXCLUSIONS
This scope of services does not include:
• Signalization
• Relocation design for any utility facilities
• Construction engineering and inspection (CEI)
• Public involvement
• Record drawings
• Preparation of easements
• Landscape design
• Polk County right-of-way (R/W) permitting.
• Geotechnical investigation or engineering
• Pedestrian signal design
IV.  CONSULTANT  COMPENSATION
The Consultant will perform the above Scope of Services on an hourly rate fee basis up to the Not to Exceed
Limit of the Authorization of $21,261.00 In the event the Scope of Services requires modifications at the
request of the County, or unforeseen circumstances require additional work, an amendment will be required
to make necessary adjustments. The fee schedule for this Scope of Services is shown in the attached Exhibit.
3

MEMORANDUM
DATE:	November 13, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commissioners
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
FROM:	Stephanie Lutton, Parks & Recreation Director
SUBJECT:  Orange Blossom Revue Funding
SYNOPSIS: The City Commission will consider authorizing the payment of $40,000 to
support Orange Blossom Revue Infrastructure needs.
RECOMMENDATION
Staff recommends that the City Commission consider the following action(s):
Authorize the city to process a payment for $40,000 to Blossom Revue Charitable
Foundation, Inc.
BACKGROUND
The Orange Blossom Revue is an annual music festival that has become a significant
cultural event for Lake Wales. The festival has grown substantially over the years,
attraction a diverse audience and contributing to the local economy. However, the event’s
increasing popularity has also led to increased infrastructure needs, including power,
fencing, and stage equipment. To ensure the continued success of the festival and its
positive impact on the community, additional funding is required to support these
infrastructure improvements.
City staff is requesting the authorization of $30,000 to the Blossom Revue Charitable
Foundation to help offset costs associated with Orange Blossom Revue held in Lake
Wailes Park on December 4-5, 2026.
City Staff is also requesting the authorization of $10,000 to the Blossom Revue Charitable
Foundation to help offset costs associated with the additional Orange Blossom Revue
event to be held in Oakley Park on Thursday December 3, 2026.
OPTIONS
1. The City Commission may choose not to grant the funds to the Orange Blossom
Revue

FISCAL IMPACT
  The payment of $40,000.

MEMORANDUM
DATE:	November 5, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commission
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
Chris Velasquez, Chief of Police
FROM:	Andrew Brown, Lieutenant
SUBJECT:  Police Department Annex Furniture Purchase
SYNOPSIS: Staff is requesting Commission approval to purchase office furniture for the
new police annex building.
RECOMMENDATION
Staff recommends the City Commission Authorize the expenditure of $99,927.60 to
purchase office furnishings from Holmes + Brakel using State of Florida purchasing
Contract FCS 56120000-24-NY-ACS.
BACKGROUND
The City Commission previously approved the purchase of the office building located at
122 E. Tillman Avenue to serve as the Police Department Annex. The purchase,
remodel and safety enhancements required to operate the building are funded through
Public Safety Loan 2026A. The project is progressing as scheduled and staff has
obtained a quote for office furnishings as the construction nears completion.
The proposed acquisition will provide the necessary furnishings to support efficient daily
operations and ensure employees have the appropriate resources to perform their
duties effectively.
OTHER OPTIONS
The City Commission may choose not to purchase these furnishings.
FISCAL IMPACT
The Police Department proposes to use funds from Public Safety Loan 2026A to
purchase these furnishings. The total cost of this expenditure is $99,927.60.

Holmes + Brakel
STANDARD  TERMS AND CONDITIONS OF SALE
DEPOSIT
A minimum deposit of 50%, or as otherwise indicated upon approved credit, is required with all contract orders
CHANGES AND CANCELLATIONS
All orders are final and binding. Requests for cancellation, changes in quantity or specifications shall be delivered to seller in writing
and are subject to seller’s ability to conform which is dependent upon factory approval. Cancellation charges shall be paid by the
buyer.
DELIVERY AND INSTALLATION
In the event that delivery and/or installation is required as a part of the proposal the following provisions will apply unless otherwise
indicated on quotation:
1. Conditions of job site – the area shall be clean, clear and free of debris prior to installation.
2. Job site services – electrical current, heat, hoisting and/or elevator service will be furnished without charge to seller.
3. Delivery during normal business hours – delivery and installation will be made during normal working hours. Additional labour costs
resulting from overtime work performed at the buyer’s request will be borne by the buyer.
4. Protection of delivered goods – goods delivered and brought on to the job site as scheduled shall be inspected and conditionally
accepted by the customer.The responsibility for the security and safeguarding of the delivered goods shall at that time pass to the
buyer.
SCHEDULE & DELAYS
Buyer to provide a required install date at time of order. Any deviations to this schedule may be subject to additional charges.
In the event that construction delays or other causes not within our control force postponement of the installation:
• An increase in the contract price will be required to address additional charges including but not limited to: Storage Fees, Double
Handling, Additional Trips, Additional Freight, and Overtime for Compressed schedule.
• The Buyer accepts and understands that the current estimated storage fee is $120 / per trailer (or part thereof) / per day for product
stored by the manufacturer. Actual storage fee rate will be confirmed upon notification of the delay.
• If the Buyer is able to accept delivery to site of the product to avoid offsite storage fees, the Buyer will accept responsibility for the
protection and security of the product.
• The Buyer agrees to approve the progress claim in the amount of 90% of product only (therefore no labour that has not been
completed) which will be payable based on the original installation date
GENERAL LIABILITY
No liability shall accrue against the seller as a result of any breach of these terms and conditions resulting from any strike, lockout,
work stoppage, accident, act of God, or other delay beyond seller’s control.
PAYMENT TERMS
Upon approved credit application terms of sale are payable net 30 days unless otherwise stated / approved in writing. Partial deliveries
are to be paid for upon presentation of invoice covering each partial shipment. Interest will be charged on overdue accounts at the rate
of 2% per month. In the case of any defective or missing pieces, payment may be withheld in an amount equal to the price of the
defective or missing pieces which have been included in the invoice, to a maximum of 10%.
If the account is placed for collection, the applicant agrees to pay all costs and expenses of collection, including but not limited to,
attorney’s fees and court fees.
OWNERSHIP
The title and ownership of the subject matter of this sale and any additions shall remain the property of the seller, until such time as full
payment is made of the purchase price and interest as herein provided, together with costs and charges.
REPOSSESSION
Should the buyer be in default hereunder, the seller may at its option repossess the said subject matter.
TARIFFS
Holmes + Brakel ("the Supplier") is not responsible for the costs of any tariffs and/or import fees that may be imposed in connection
with delivery of goods to the Client. Tariffs may be applicable. For clarity, any such fees, if imposed, are the sole responsibility of the
Client and will not be covered by Holmes + Brakel. Holmes + Brakel is not liable for any costs related to customs or importation fees,
and any such fees will be billed directly to the Client.
The acceptance by Holmes & Brakel of any order is conditional upon the Applicants (the BUYER) assent, which is hereby given that
the terms and conditions hereof and/or any order acknowledgment and/or any invoice shall constitute the applicable terms and
conditions of sale which shall override any purchase order given by the BUYER.
All quotations, acknowledgements and invoices are subject to corrections for any errors and omissions. The purchaser
acknowledges that there are no verbal or other arrangements or understandings relating to this contract.
Signature

MEMORANDUM
DATE:	December 3, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commission
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
FROM:	Jennifer Nanek, City Clerk
RE:	Special Event Permit Application - 2027 Lake Wales Mardi Gras Celebration,
Parade, Fireworks. Temporary Street Closings and Alcohol Use
SYNOPSIS
Approval of the Special Event Permit Application for the Lake Wales Mardi Gras Celebration,
Parade, and Fireworks on Friday, February 5, 2027, and Saturday, February 6, 2027.
RECOMMENDATION
It is recommended that the City Commission take the following actions:
•  Approve the Special Event Permit application for the 2027 Lake Wales Mardi Gras
Celebration to take place on Friday evening, February 5, 2027, from 5:00 p.m. until 11:00
p.m., and on Saturday, February 6, 2027, from 11:00 a.m. until 11:00 p.m. in Lake
Wailes Park, with set-up beginning at 8:00 a.m. on Thursday, February 4, 2027, and
takedown concluding by 12:00 p.m. on Sunday, February 7, 2027.
•  Approve the serving and consumption of alcohol in designated areas of Lake Wailes Park
during the Mardi Gras Event.
•  Approve the temporary street and alley closures on Saturday, February 6, 2027, for the
Mardi Gras parade, specifically detailing the following segments:
Line-up: First Street (between 1st and Wetmore).
o
Route: East on Park Avenue to Scenic Highway (SR 17), South on Scenic
o
Highway to Stuart Avenue, West on Stuart Avenue to First Street, South on First
Street to Central Avenue, East on Central Avenue to Lake Shore Boulevard, and
North on Lake Shore Boulevard through Lake Wailes Park, along with all
associated connecting alleys and intersections along Central Avenue, Park
Avenue, Stuart Avenue, Scenic Highway, and Lake Shore Boulevard.
BACKGROUND
Joshua Curchy, President of the Lake Wales Mardi Gras, has submitted a Special Event Permit
Application for the 2027 Lake Wales Mardi Gras Celebration, Parade, and Fireworks to be held
Friday, February 5, 2027, and Saturday, February 6, 2027, in Lake Wailes Park.

The festival activities—including food and craft vendors, live musical entertainment, a children’s
area, and alcohol sales—will take place in Lake Wailes Park. The parade will route through
downtown streets.
Activities for the 2027 Lake Wales Mardi Gras are proposed as follows:
•  Thursday, February 4, 2027 (8:00 a.m.): Event setup begins in Lake Wailes Park.
•  Friday, February 5, 2027 (5:00 p.m. to 11:00 p.m.): Food and craft vendors, live
music, children's area, and alcohol sales in Lake Wailes Park. Fireworks display
(approximately 7 to 8 minutes) will take place in the evening.
•  Saturday, February 6, 2027 (11:00 a.m. to 11:00 p.m.): Festival activities continue in
Lake Wailes Park with food/craft vendors, kids' activities, live music, and alcohol sales.
All activities conclude at 11:00 p.m.
•  Saturday, February 6, 2027 (Parade): Floats line up on Park Avenue. The parade
begins at the intersection of 1st Street and Park Avenue, heading east on Park Avenue,
looping south, traveling west along Stuart Avenue, turning south onto 1st Street, heading
east along Central Avenue, turning north onto Lake Shore Boulevard, and concluding at
Lake Wailes Park.
The Police Department and Streets Department will coordinate vehicular and pedestrian traffic
control for the parade. The Streets Department will handle street closures. Sanitary facilities,
dumpsters, site setup, tent permits, certificates of liability insurance ($1,000,000 naming the City
as additional insured), FDOT road closing permits, and the alcoholic beverage permit from the
Division of Alcoholic Beverages and Tobacco will be the responsibility of the Lake Wales Mardi
Gras Executive Committee. Off-duty officers required for police details will be paid by the event
sponsor.
OTHER  OPTIONS
Do not approve the two-day event, fireworks, or temporary street closures.
FISCAL IMPACT
In-Kind services from the City are budgeted for FY 2026/2027. The Lake Wales Mardi Gras
Committee will be responsible for their required percentage reimbursement per City policy. Any
off-duty police officers requested will be paid at 100% by the sponsor.
ATTACHMENTS
•  Special Event Permit Application and Agreement of Financial Responsibility
•  Parade Lineup Map
•  Site Layout Map

November 5, 2026
City of Lake Wales
Jennifer Nanek, City Clerk
Special Event Review Staff
201 West Central Avenue
Lake Wales, FL 33853
2027 Lake  Wales Mardi Gras Event Details and Special Requests
The Lake Wales Mardi Gras Festival and Celebration will take place on February 5-6, 2027.
The Lake Wales Mardi Gras parade will be held on Saturday, February 6, 2027. The event
festival will take place in Lake Wailes Park. Event details and special requests are as follows.
THURSDAY, FEBRUARY 4, 2027
Mardi Gras committee members will be present by 8:00 a.m. Republic Service will deliver
the dumpsters to Lake Wailes Park. Rhodes will be delivering the sanitary facilities as well
to the park and along the parade route. Premier Parties will be delivering and setting up
three 20x40 tents to the event site and delivering all the chairs and tables. Note: LWMG will
be responsible for applying for the tent permit form to the city.
FRIDAY, FEBRUARY 5, 2027
1. Mardi Gras Executive Committee and volunteers will be setting up the VIP area, tables
and chairs, smaller tents, kid’s craft areas and decorating starting at 8:00 a.m.
2. The stage will be delivered and set up.
3. Ice trailer, generators and beer trailer will be delivered and set up.
FRIDAY NIGHT ACTIVITIES
1. Mardi Gras activities Friday night in Lake Wailes park will begin at 5:00 p.m. and end
at 11:00 pm.
2. There will be a VIP area set up for those purchasing VIP tickets and our sponsors.
3. Beer sales will start at 6:00 p.m. and will stop at 10:30 p.m.
4. As part of the special event permit, LWMG Executive Board WILL NOT ALLOW the
public to bring in personal coolers to the event Friday or Saturday.
5. Bands will be performing on stage during the event.
6. Food and craft vendors will be set up inside the event area.
SPECIAL SECURITY – Lake Wales Mardi Gras will hire off duty officers for special security
detail Friday night, Friday overnight, Saturday and Saturday overnight.
P. O. BOX 3631 I LAKE WALES, FL 33859 I www,LWMardiGras.com

SATURDAY, FEBRUARY 6, 2027
8:00 a.m. – The remaining vendors will arrive and set up in Lake Wailes Park. All vendors
must be in place by 10:00 a.m.
Mardi Gras Event – The event will continue on Saturday in Lake Wailes Park. Activities will
begin at 11:30 a.m. and will conclude at 11:00 p.m. Beer sales will stop at 10:30 p.m.
Fireworks – A seven to eight minute fireworks show will be presented at 8:15 p.m. south of
the event site at Lake Wailes Park.
Mardi Gras Parade – The parade will begin at 3:00 p.m. and conclude at approximately
5:00 p.m. Lineup will be on Park Avenue. We request that the areas for the lineup be closed
by 11:00 a.m.
We are requesting the following streets be closed for the parade route:
Parade will line up on Park Avenue beginning at First Street and heading west down park.
The parade will turn right on SR 17 to Stuart Aven, turn right on Stuart Avenue to First
Street, turn left on First Street to Central Avenue, turn left on Central Avenue and travel to
Lakeshore Boulevard and Lake Wales Park where the parade will conclude.
SPECIAL REQUESTS
LWPD
1. We request the assistance of the LWPD on Friday night, Saturday day and evening for
the event at the park and parade.
2. Mardi Gras will hire off duty officers for special security detail for Friday and Saturday.
3. We request the assistance of LWPD officers to stop the public from bringing in
personal coolers to the event site on Friday and Saturday. (LWMG is not allowing any
coolers from the public to be brought into the event area.)
4. We request LWPD to set up two light towers in Lake Wailes Park.
5. We request that the officers working the parade on Saturday do not rush the parade
entries as many will have Krewe members walking in the parade.
FIRE DEPARTMENT
We request the Lake Wales Fire Department be on site on Friday and Saturday to provide
first aid assistance to the public.
P. O. BOX 3631 I LAKE WALES, FL 33859 I www,LWMardiGras.com

STREET DEPARTMENT
1. We request the assistance of the Street Department in closing off streets for the
parade line up and the parade itself.
2. We request assistance for the clean up of trash along the parade route.
3. We request additional barricades in Lake Wailes Park to close off the two main paved
entrance on Friday morning, February 5, 2027 by 7:00 a.m. to help prevent non-event
vehicular traffic from entering.
PARKS DEPARTMENT
1. We request that a staff member stops by Lake Wailes Park while all the deliveries
are being done on Thursday morning for sanitary facilities, dumpsters and the tents,
tables and chairs.
2. We request that any hanging limps be trimmed back at the VIP tent area and at the
stage site.
3. We request assistance with the cleanup of trash in Lake Wailes Park event area and
pick up of flattened boxes form the VIP site on Friday night and Saturday day and
evening. (20 yd. container will be set up for the trash clean up in the same area as last
year)
4. On the chance that the weather is very cold, we ask for the opportunity to use the
city’s propane heaters in our event area.
The Lake Wales Mardi Gras Executive Committee sincerely appreciates the support and
cooperation from the City of Lake Wales each year for the event. We appreciate the staff
from the LWPD, LWFD, Street Department and Parks Department who work so closely with
the LWMG Committee to ensure that any issues are addressed and resolved.
Lake Wales Mardi Gras will proceed with obtaining the alcohol permit, tent permit and
obtaining the required liability insurance documents.
Thank you,
Joshua Curchy
LW Mardi Gras President
321.615.5183
joshcurchy@gmail.com
P. O. BOX 3631 I LAKE WALES, FL 33859 I www,LWMardiGras.com

MEMORANDUM
DATE:	November 10, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commission
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
FROM:	Jennifer Nanek, City Clerk
RE:	Event Permit Application: Christmas Cinema with Santa on December 19, 2026.
SYNOPSIS
This is a request from the City of Lake Wales Recreation, to host an event at Oakley Park called
Christmas Cinema with Santa on Saturday December 19, 2026. This event will involve closure of
Cypress Garden Lane and a portion of Crystal Avenue from 2:00 p.m. – 8:00 p.m.
RECOMMENDATION
Staff recommends
1. That the City Commission approve the permit request to sponsor a Christmas Cinema with
Santa event on Saturday December 19th from 4:00 p.m. – 8:00 p.m.
2. That the City Commission approve the closure of Cypress Garden Lane.
BACKGROUND
The City of Lake Wales is proposing to host a Christmas Cinema with Santa event on Saturday
December 19th from 4:00 p.m. – 8:00 p.m. in Oakley Park. The event will feature closing Cypress
Gardens Ln. The Cypress Garden Lane West parking lot will also be closed.
OTHER OPTIONS
Do not approve the Special Event Permit.
FISCAL IMPACT
Unknown.

Special Event Permit application

MEMORANDUM
DATE:	November 10, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commission
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
Sarah B. Kirkland, Public Works Director
FROM:	Holly Britt, Utilities Operations Manager
SUBJECT: Approval of Proposed Verizon Wireless Water Tower Lease Terms – Contract No.
50502
SYNOPSIS: Staff recommends City Commission approval of the revised lease terms proposed by
Verizon Wireless for its communications equipment located on the City's water tower at
Sessoms and Market Street. Verizon has proposed increasing the monthly lease payment to
$4,000.00, effective July 1, 2027, with a 15% rental increase every five years and five additional
five-year renewal terms.
STAFF RECOMMENDATION
Staff recommends that the City Commission consider taking the following action:
1. Approve the revised lease terms proposed by Verizon Wireless for the City's water tower
communications site, Contract No. 50502.
2. Authorize the City Manager to execute a lease amendment incorporating the approved
terms.
BACKGROUND
The City of Lake Wales entered into a Water Tower Lease Agreement with Verizon Wireless
Personal Communications LP, doing business as Verizon Wireless, in 2002. The agreement allows
Verizon Wireless to install, maintain, and operate telecommunications equipment on the City's
water tower located at Sessoms and Market Street.
The existing lease provides Verizon Wireless with space on the water tower for communications
equipment, along with an equipment area and access and utility easements necessary to
operate and maintain the facility.
Verizon Wireless has contacted the City regarding modifications to the existing lease agreement.
The purpose of the proposed modifications is to extend the lease and allow Verizon to retain
the site as part of its long-term communications network.
In a letter dated June 11, 2026, Verizon Wireless presented the following proposed terms for
the City's consideration:

Proposed Lease Modifications
•  New Monthly Rental Amount: $4,000.00 per month.
•  Effective Date of New Rent: July 1, 2027.
•  Rental Escalation: A 15% increase every five (5) years, with the first increase scheduled
for July 1, 2032.
•  Additional Renewal Terms: Five (5) additional renewal terms of five (5) years each.
Under the proposed rental arrangement, the City would receive $48,000.00 annually beginning
July 1, 2027, prior to the scheduled five-year rental increases.
Verizon Wireless has indicated that these modifications will allow the company to continue
utilizing the City's water tower location to meet its current operational needs and provide long-
term value to its communications network.
The proposal states that it does not constitute a binding offer to amend the existing lease. No
legal obligation will be created until a written lease amendment has been prepared and
executed by both the City and Verizon Wireless. Until that time, the existing lease terms will
remain in effect.
Staff is presenting the proposed terms for City Commission consideration and approval so that
the necessary lease amendment can be finalized.
OTHER OPTIONS
The City Commission may elect to reject the proposed lease modifications or direct staff to
negotiate different terms with Verizon Wireless. The existing lease would remain in effect unless
amended, terminated, or otherwise expired in accordance with its provisions.
FISCAL IMPACT
The proposed lease modification would generate rental revenue of $4,000.00 per month, or
$48,000.00 annually, beginning July 1, 2027.
The proposed agreement also provides for a 15% rental increase every five years, beginning July
1, 2032. This would provide the City with additional recurring revenue throughout the proposed
renewal
ATTACHMENTS
Existing Verizon Lease
Verizon Term Sheet
New Verizon Lease

equipment, antennas and appurtenances described in Exhibit "B" attached hereto.
LESSEE reserves the right to replace the aforementioned equipment with similar and comparable equipment
provided said replacement does not increase tower loading of said Tower.

however, the initial tenn shall be for five (5) years and shall commence on the Commencement Date (as hereinafter

($22,800.00) to be paid in equal monthly installments on the first day of the month, in advance, to the City of Lake
Wales or to such other person, firm or place as the LESSOR may, from time to time, designate in writing at least thirty

terms unless the LESSEE terminates it at the end of the then current term by giving the LESSOR written notice of the
intent to terminate at least six ( 6) months prior to the end of the then current term.

to the immediately preceding five (5) year term.
7.  USE; GOVERNMENTAL APPROVALS. LESSEE shall use the Premises for the purpose of
constructing, maintaining and operating a communications facility and uses incidental and all necessary appurtenances.

LESSEE shall have the right to replace, repair, add or otherwise modify its equipment or any portion thereof, whether
the equipment is specified or not on any exhibit attached hereto, during the term of this Agreement. LESSEE will
maintain the Premises in a good condition reasonable wear and tear excepted. LESSOR will maintain the Property,

as set forth above. LESSOR shall cooperate with LESSEE in its effort to obtain such approvals and shall take no action

found to be unsatisfactory so that LESSEE in its sole discretion will be unable to use the Premises for its intended
purposes or the LESSEE determines that the Property is no longer technically compatible for its intended use, LESSEE
shall have the right to terminate this Agreement. Notice of the LESSEE's exercise of its right to terminate shall be given
to LESSOR in writing by certified mail, return receipt requested, and shall be effective upon the mailing of such notice
by the LESSEE. All rentals paid to said termination date shall be retained by the LESSOR. Upon such termination,
this Agreement shall become null and void and all the Parties shall have no further obligations including the payment of
money, to each other.
8.	ACCESS TO TOWER. LESSOR agrees the LESSEE shall have free access to the Tower at all
times for the purpose of installing and maintaining the said equipment. LESSOR shall furnish LESSEE with necessary
means of access for the purpose of ingress and egress to this site and Tower location. It is agreed, however, that only
authorized engineers, employees or properly authorized contractors of LESSEE or persons under their direct supervision
will be perrnitted to enter said premises.

by all federal, state, county and local laws. The LESSOR shall also comply with all rules and regulations enforced by the

may self-insure against any loss or damage which could be covered by a comprehensive general public liability
insurance policy.

provided that three (3) months prior notice is given the LESSOR.

whatsoever. Each party covenants to indemnify and hold the other party harmless from all claims, demands,
damages, fines, costs, cleanup, attorney's fees, and court costs (collectively, "Claims") arising from the indemnitor's
material misrepresentations or from existing or future discharge (either intentionally or accidentally) of such matters
on the Premises caused by the indernnitor or its predecessors in interest, agents, licensees or assigns.
It is possible that during LESSEE's installation of its Communications Equipment, the lead based paint on
the Tower will be disturbed. In such event, LESSEE and its contractors will be responsible for taking reasonable
steps to protect themselves (as well as the surrounding area/people) from any lead based paint that is "released"
during the Communications Equipment installation process. In regard to any lead paint that must be removed from
the Tower for installation of LESSEE's equipment (except in the case where LESSEE was grossly negligent during
such removal), or excavated media that requires off-site disposal because it is not suitable to be returned to the
ground from which it was removed pursuant to any law, LESSOR shall execute any and all manifests for such
disposal as, and hereby acknowledges that it is, the "owner" and "generator" of such waste or contaminated media,
and that it is the "arranger" for such disposal. LESSOR will not be responsible for the actual cost of disposal, which
shall be borne by LESSEE.
LESSOR also indemnifies and holds harmless LESSEE in regard to any Claims brought against LESSEE
that alleges that LESSEE is liable for (I) any of LESSOR's past, present or future uses, actions, equipment, or
occupancy of the Premises or any use of or impact to any of the surrounding property, all as regarding any releases
or threats of release of, or the disposal of, lead-based paint to or in, the environment, where such lead based paint
came to be located in the environment due to the actions of LESSOR or its predecessors in interest, agents, assigns
or contractors; and (2) any theory that LESSEE liable as the "owner, operator or lessee of', or in any way
responsible for, any condition of pollution at the Premises or surrounding area due to its being contaminated by lead
based paint, or that LESSEE exacerbated any existing pollution or disposal, or caused a release, by working in the
soils and groundwater of the Premises or the surrounding area. Further, LESSEE's indemnification of LESSOR
specifically excludes any Claims brought against or by LESSOR where there is any allegation that LESSEE (1)
released, disposed of or caused pollution or other environmental impact or (2) is strictly liable for environmental
conditions at the Premises or in the surrounding area or (3) is responsible for any legal violation, as to all of the
preceding due to: LESSEE's mere presence on the Premises as a LESSEE, or LESSEE's excavations of soil, or the
handling or disposal of soil or groundwater at the Premises or the presence of such soil or groundwater at any remote
disposal location, including the handling of soil or groundwater in access areas and trenching areas, unless said soil
or groundwater was impacted by substances other than lead-based paint, and which substances LESSEE or its agents
brought onto, or caused to be brought onto and subsequently released to or disposed on, the Premises.
29.  CASUALTY. In the event of damage by fire or other casualty to the Premises that cannot
reasonably be expected to be repaired within forty-five (45) days following same or, if the Property is damaged by fire
or other casualty so that such damage may reasonably be expected to disrupt LESSEE's operations at the Premises for
more than forty-five (45) days, then LESSEE may at any time following such fire or other casualty, provided LESSOR
has not completed the restoration required to permit LESSEE to resume its operation at the Premises, terminate this
Lease upon fifteen (15) days written notice to LESSOR. Any such notice of termination shall cause this Lease to expire
with the same force and effect as though the date set forth in such notice were the date originally set as the expiration
date of this Lease and the parties shall make an appropriate adjustment, as of such termination date, with respect to
payments due to the other under this Lease. Notwithstanding the foregoing, all rental shall abate during the period of
such fire or other casualty.
30.  CONDEMNATION.  In the event ofany condemnation of the Property, LESSEE may tenninate
this Lease upon fifteen (15) days written notice to LESSOR if such condemnation may reasonably be expected to disrupt
LESSEE's operations at the Premises for more than forty-five (45) days. LESSEE may on its own behalf make a claim
in any condemnation proceeding involving the Premises for losses related to the antennas, equipment, its relocation costs
6

Lessee Site ID: Lake Wales Water Tower 82165-4
and its damages and losses (but not for the loss of its leasehold interest). Any such notice of termination shall cause this
Lease to expire with the same force and effect as though the date set forth in such notice were the date originally set as
the expiration date of this Lease and the parties shall make an appropriate adjustment as of such termination date with
respect to payments due to the other under this Lease.
31.  SUBMISSION OF LEASE. The submission of this Lease for examination does not constitute
an offer to lease the Premises and this Lease becomes effective only upon the full execution of this Lease by the Parties.
If any provision herein is invalid, it shall be considered deleted from this Lease and shall not invalidate the remaining
provisions of this Lease. Each of the Parties hereto warrants to the other that the person or persons executing this Lease
on behalf of such party has the full right, power and authority to enter into and execute this Lease on such Party's behalf
and that no consent from any other person or entity is necessary as a condition precedent to the legal effect of this Lease.
32.  APPLICABLE LAWS. LESSEE shall use the Premises as may be required or as permitted by
• applicable laws, rules and regulations. LESSOR agrees to keep the Property in conformance with all applicable,
laws, rules and regulations and agrees to reasonably cooperate with the LESSEE regarding any compliance required
by the LESSEE in respect to its use of the Premises.
33.  SURVIVAL. The provisions of the Agreement relating to indemnification from one Party to the
other Party shall survive any termination or expiration of this Agreement. Additionally, any provisions of this
Agreement which require performance subsequent to the termination or expiration of this Agreement shall also survive
such termination or expiration.
34.  CAPTIONS. The captions contained in this Agreement are inserted for convenience only and are
not intended to be part of the Agreement. They shall not affect or be utilized in the construction or interpretation of the
Agreement.

MEMORANDUM
DATE: November 10, 2026
TO:  Honorable Mayor and City Commission
VIA: James Slaton, City Manager
Sarah B. Kirkland, Public Works Director
FROM: Holly Britt, Utilities Operations Manager
SUBJECT: Eagle Ridge Mall Area and Southside Force Main Cleaning
SYNOPSIS: Staff recommends approval of an expenditure totaling $139,264.92 to Professional
Piping Services for the cleaning of two separate wastewater force mains: the Eagle Ridge Mall
Area Force Main and the Southside Force Main. Both cleaning projects will be performed
utilizing the City of Ormond Beach piggyback agreement, which was previously approved by the
City Commission on January 20, 2026.
STAFF RECOMMENDATION
Staff recommends that the City Commission take the following action:
1. Approve the expenditure of $139,264.92 to Professional Piping Services for the cleaning
of the Eagle Ridge Mall Area Force Main and the Southside Force Main, utilizing the City
of Ormond Beach piggyback agreement previously approved by the City Commission on
January 20, 2026.
2. Authorize the City Manager to execute any necessary documents associated with the
services.
BACKGROUND
The City's wastewater collection system includes multiple force mains that require periodic
cleaning to maintain adequate flow and prevent the accumulation of sediment and debris. Two
force mains have been identified for cleaning: the Eagle Ridge Mall Area Force Main and the
Southside Force Main. Both lines require cleaning due to their distance from the wastewater
treatment plant, low-flow conditions, and, in the case of the Southside Force Main, significant
topographical changes within the service area.
Eagle Ridge Mall Area Force Main
The Eagle Ridge Mall Area Force Main serves Chalet Suzanne, Dinner Lake, Lake Ashton, and
Harding Avenue. Due to the distance wastewater must travel to reach the wastewater
treatment plant and the low-flow conditions that occur during non-peak hours, sediment and
debris such as sand, plastics, grease, and paper can settle at the bottom of the pipe. Over time,
this material can accumulate and restrict wastewater flow, potentially resulting in blockages

that could disrupt the collection system and interrupt sewer service to the businesses and
residential subdivisions within the service area.
The proposed cleaning method is known as the progressive poly pig procedure, which is
designed to remove accumulated sediment and debris from the force main.
Professional Piping Services has performed this cleaning for the City over the past several years.
Staff has been satisfied with the company's performance and the reports submitted following
the cleaning, which are a required component of the service.
The quoted cost for cleaning the Eagle Ridge Mall Area Force Main is $87,865.32.
Southside Force Main
The Southside Force Main also requires periodic cleaning due to the distance wastewater must
travel to reach the wastewater treatment plant, low-flow conditions, and significant
topographical changes throughout the service area.
These conditions contribute to reduced flow velocities, allowing sediment and debris, including
sand, plastics, and paper, to settle and accumulate within the pipe. If not removed, this material
can restrict wastewater flow and potentially lead to blockages or interruptions in sewer service.
The proposed cleaning method for this force main is also the progressive poly pig procedure,
which is intended to remove accumulated debris and maintain the operational efficiency of the
wastewater collection system.
The quoted cost for cleaning the Southside Force Main is $51,399.60.
Contracting and Procurement
The City has historically solicited bids for force main cleaning services; however, the number of
responsive bidders has decreased over the years. To address this need, the City is utilizing an
existing competitively procured agreement awarded by the City of Ormond Beach.
The City Commission approved the piggyback agreement with the City of Ormond Beach on
January 20, 2026, authorizing the City to utilize the agreement for these services.
Professional Piping Services has experience performing force main cleaning for the City, and
staff has been satisfied with the company's work and the required post-cleaning reports.
Staff recommends utilizing the previously approved piggyback agreement to complete both
cleaning projects at the same time. Coordinating the work will allow the City to address the
maintenance needs of both force mains while utilizing the same contractor and agreement.
The combined cost for both cleaning projects is $139,264.92.
OTHER OPTIONS

None recommended. Without periodic cleaning, sediment and debris can accumulate within
the force mains, increasing the potential for blockages and restricting wastewater flow. A
blockage could result in interruptions to sewer service for the residential and commercial
customers served by these collection systems.
FISCAL IMPACT
A total of $225,000.00 is budgeted in the Wastewater Division's FY 2026–2027 Maintenance and
Repair (M&R) Lines and Mains Operating Budget for this purpose.
The combined expenditure of $139,264.92 will be paid from this budget, leaving an estimated
remaining balance of $85,735.08.
ATTACHMENTS
1. Professional Piping Services Quote No. 1026-3219 – Eagle Ridge Mall Area Force Main
Cleaning ($87,865.32)
2. Professional Piping Services Quote – Southside Force Main Cleaning ($51,399.60)
3. City of Ormond Beach Piggyback Agreement

AGREEMENT TO PIGGYBACK A CONTRACT FOR SERVICES
BID BY ANOTHER GOVERNMENTAL ENTITY
WHEREAS, Professional Piping Services, Inc. , a Florida corporation (the "Contractor"), entered into an
agreement dated July 2025 and ending July 2028 with City of Ormond Beach , a
political subdivision of the State of Florida, for Forcemain cleaning procured pursuant to
F.S. §287.057 (the "Contract"),
WHEREAS, the CITY OF LAKE WALES, a Florida municipal corporation (the "City") has the legal
authority under Ordinance 2013-17 adopted by the Lake Wales City Commission to "piggyback"
onto a contract procured pursuant to F.S. §287.057 by another governmental entity when seeking
to utilize the same or similar services provided for in the said contract; and
WHEREAS, the City desires to "piggyback" onto the above referenced Contract between the Contractor
and City of Ormond Beach for utilization of the same or similar services for Forcemain cleaning (the
"Work") and the Contractor consents to the aforesaid "piggybacking".
NOW THEREFORE, having found it to be in the public interest,
1. The Contractor affirms and ratifies the terms and conditions of the above referenced Contract with _g_ty_
of Ormond Beach and agrees to perform the services set forth therein for the City in accordance with the
terms of said Contract until the Work is completed. Contractor further agrees that for the purposes of
interpretation and enforcement of the subject Contract, the term "City of Lake Wales" shall be substituted
for the term "City of Ormond Beach".
2. The City agrees to utilize the services of the Contractor in a manner and upon the terms and conditlons
as set forth in the City of Ormond Beach Agreement until the Work is completed.
A. Public Records. Contractor acknowledges that it is acting on behalf of a Public agency and that this
Agreement is s.ubject to the provisions of § 119. 0701, Florida Statutes, and that Contractor must comply
with the public records laws of the State of Florida.
Contractor shall:
1. Keep and maintain public records required by the public agency to perform the service.
2. Upon request from the public agency's custodian of public records, the Contractor shall provide the
public agency with a copy of the requested records or allow the records to be inspected or copied within a
reasonable time at a cost that does not exceed the cost provided in this chapter or as otherwise provided
by law.
3. The Contractor shall ensure that public records that are exempt or confidential and exempt from public
records disclosure requirements are not disclosed except as authorized by law for the duration of the
contract term and following completion of the contract if the contractor does not transfer the records to the
public agency.
4, The Contractor shall, upon completion of the contract, transfer, at no cost, to the public agency all public

records in possession of the Contractor or keep and maintain public records required by the publicagency
to perform the service. If the Contractor transfers all public records to the public agency upon completion
of the contract, the Contractor shall destroy any duplicate public records that are exempt or confidential and
exempt from public records disclosure requirements. If the Contractor keeps and maintains public records
upon completion of the contract, the Contractor shall meet all applicable requirements for retaining public
records. All records stored electronically must be provided to the public agency, upon request from the
public agency's custodian of public records, in a format that is compatible with the information technology
systems of the public agency.
5. A request to inspect or copy public records relating to a public agency's contract for services must be
made directly to the public agency. If the public agency does not possess the requested records, the public
agency shall immediately notify the Contractor of the request, and the Contractor must provide the records
to the public agency or allow the records to be inspecte~ or copied within a reasonable time.
6. If Contractor does not comply with a public agency's request for records, the public agency shall
enforce the contract provisions in accordance with the contract.
7. A Contractor who fails to provide the public records to the public agency within a reasonable time may
be subject to penalties under s. 119.10.
8. If a civil action is filed against a Contractor to compel production of public records relating to a public
agency's contract for services, the court shall assess and award against the Contractor the reasonable
costs of enforcement, including reasonable attorney fees, if:
(a) The court determines that the Contractor unlawfully refused to comply with the public records
request within a reasonable time; and
(b) At least 8 business days before filing the action, the plaintiff provided written notice of the public
records request, including a statement that the Contractor has not complied with the request, to the
public agency and to the Contra.c~or.
9. A notice complies with subparagraph (8)b if it is sent to the public agency's custodian of public records
and to the Contractor at the Contractor's address listed on its contract with the public agency or to the
Contractor's registered agent. Such notices must be sent by common carrier delivery service or by
registered, Global Express Guaranteed, or certified mail, with postage or shipping paid by the sender and
with evidence of delivery, which may be in an electronic format.
10. A Contractor who complies with a public records request within 8 business days after the notice is sent
is not liable for the reasonable costs of enforcement.

MEMORANDUM
DATE:	November 2, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commission
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
Sarah B. Kirkland, Public Works Director
FROM:	Margarita Dimaz, Utilities Support Manager
RE:	Hunt Brothers Road Utility Improvements Project- Utility Easement Valuations
and Expenditure Approval
SYNOPSIS: Request City Commission approval of an expenditure not to exceed $211,000 for
the acquisition associated with the Hunt Brothers Road Utility Improvements
Project.
RECOMMENDATION
It is recommended that the Commission take the following action:
1. Staff recommends that the City Commission approve an expenditure not to exceed
$211,000.00 for the acquisition of utility easements from Hunt Brothers, Inc., Story
Groves, Inc., and McKenna Brothers, Inc., to facilitate the continuation of the Hunt
Brothers Road Utility Improvements Project.
2. Authorize the City Manager to execute the appropriate documents on the City’s behalf.
BACKGROUND
The City of Lake Wales is preparing to move forward with the Hunt Brothers Road Utility
Improvements Project. To proceed with the project, the City must obtain the necessary utility
easements from the affected property owners.
Valuations have been established for the utility easements associated with the following property
owners:
1. Hunt Brothers, Inc. – $173,400.00
2. Story Groves, Inc. – $17,200.00
3. McKenna Brothers, Inc. – $20,400.00
The combined estimated expenditure for the three utility easements is $211,000.00. Obtaining the
necessary easements is an important step toward advancing the Hunt Brothers Road Utility
Improvements Project and allowing the City to proceed with the proposed utility improvements.

City Commission approval is requested to authorize the expenditure necessary to proceed with the
utility easement acquisition process for the identified properties. Staff recommends that the City
Commission approve an expenditure not to exceed $211,000.00 for the acquisition of utility
easements from Hunt Brothers, Inc., Story Groves, Inc., and McKenna Brothers, Inc., to facilitate
the continuation of the Hunt Brothers Road Utility Improvements Project.
OTHER OPTIONS
1. Approve the expenditure: Authorize the expenditure not to exceed $211,000.00 to
proceed with the utility easement acquisition process and advance the Hunt Brothers
Road Utility Improvements Project.
2. Direct staff to negotiate revised valuations: Request that staff coordinate with the affected
property owners to evaluate potential adjustments to the proposed easement
compensation amounts.
3. Do not approve the expenditure: The City would be unable to proceed with the proposed
easement acquisitions under the requested authorization, potentially delaying the Hunt
Brothers Road Utility Improvements Project while staff evaluates alternative options.
FISCAL IMPACT
The funds of $211,000 for additional easement will come from the FY’26-’27 Capital budget line
item for Force main Extensions.
ATTACHMENTS
•  Hunt Brothers Road Utility Easement Valuation
•  Story Groves, Inc. Utility Easement Valuation
•  McKenna Brothers, Inc. Utility Easement Valuation

Hunt Brothers Road Utility Easement Valuation
Hunt Bros., Inc:
3,569.94 ft x 15 = 53,549.10 ft
53,549.10 ft / 43,560 = 1.23 acres
1.23 acres x $80,000 = $98,400
Approximately number of trees to be removed:
The value of the trees can potentially be reduced by careful coordination of the installation
of the pipeline
150 trees x $500= $75,000
Hunt Bros., Inc. Total= $173,400

MEMORANDUM
November 17, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commission
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
FROM:	Dorothy Abbott, Finance Director
RE:	Ordinance 2026-40, FY25’26 Budget Amendment #1, 2nd Reading & Public
Hearing
SYNOPSIS: The City Commission will consider approval of the first amendment of FY 25’26
Budget that was adopted on September 16, 2025.
RECOMMENDATION
It is recommended that the City Commission adopt Ordinance 2026-40 after second reading and
public hearing.
The City Commission approved first reading of the ordinance on November 3, 2026. The
advertisement requirement for second reading and public reading has been met.
BACKGROUND
Ordinance 2025-05 estimating revenues and appropriating funds for Fiscal Year 2025’26 was
adopted by the City Commission September 16, 2025.
We are presenting Ordinance 2026-40 to modify the estimates of revenues and appropriations
budgeted in various funds. This is primarily a housekeeping ordinance to conform the adopted
budget to realized or expected changes within the current fiscal year. Explanation of change, for
significant items has been provided on both Exhibit A and B.
OTHER OPTIONS
This is a required budgetary amendment for compliance purposes relating to the City’s Charter
and Florida State Statute.
Per the Lake Wales City Charter, Section 6.07, upon written request by the City Manager, the City
Commission may by ordinance transfer part or all of any unencumber appropriation balance from
one department, office or agency to another. Per Florida State Statute, Section 166.241, the budget
must regulate expenditures of the municipality. If a budget amendment is required, the amendment
must be adopted in the same manner as the original budget.
FISCAL IMPACT
See Exhibit A and Exhibit B attached to Ordinance 2026-40
ATTACHMENTS
Ordinance 2026-40; Exhibit A and Exhibit B.

ORDINANCE 2026-40
AN ORDINANCE OF THE CITY OF LAKE WALES, POLK COUNTY, FLORIDA, AMENDING
ORDINANCE 2025-05 TO MODIFY THE ESTIMATES OF EXPENDITURES FOR THE
OPERATION OF THE SEVERAL DEPARTMENTS OF SAID CITY FOR THE 2025-2026 FISCAL
YEAR AND TO MODIFY THE APPROPRIATION OF FUNDS RAISED AND COLLECTED
FROM ALL SOURCES SET FORTH IN THE ESTIMATE OF REVENUES FOR SAID FISCAL
YEAR; REPEALING ALL ORDINANCES IN CONFLICT WITH THIS ORDINANCE; AND
PROVIDING FOR AN EFFECTIVE DATE.
SECTION 1. Ordinance 2025-05 is hereby amended to modify the estimates of anticipated revenues for
the Fiscal Year 2025-2026 which are hereunto attached as Exhibit "A" and made a part of
this ordinance. Such anticipated revenue of said City is hereby appropriated for the several
items set forth in the budget and is hereby appropriated proportionately in the amounts as
shown in the schedule hereto attached, which said amounts are to be appropriated and
allocated to the different funds as collected.
SECTION 2. Ordinance 2025-05 is hereby amended to modify the estimates of expenditures
contemplated for the operation of the several departments of the City of Lake Wales, Polk
County, Florida, for the Fiscal Year 2025-2026 which are hereunto attached as Exhibit "B”
and made a part of this ordinance. Such amended expenditures shall be and the same are
hereby ratified, approved, confirmed, accepted and adopted as the Budget for said Fiscal
Year.
SECTION 3. All other parts of Ordinance 2025-05 remain unchanged and in full force and effect.
SECTION 4. This ordinance shall take effect immediately upon its passage and approval.
CERTIFIED AS TO PASSAGE this   day of  , 2026.
Mayor/Commissioner
ATTEST:
City Clerk

MEMORANDUM
DATE:	November 17, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commissioners
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
FROM:	Autumn Cochella, AICP, Growth Management Director
Shannon Hancock, Development Coordinator
SUBJECT:  Ordinance 2026-35 Future Land Use Map Amendment to assign .5+/- acres
RO Residential Office – 1st Reading
Public Hearing Required – Notice Requirements have been met
Ordinance 2026-36 Zoning Map Amendment to assign .5+- acres of land
PF Professional – 1st Reading
Public Hearing Required – Notice Requirements have been met
SYNOPSIS: Catherine Coyle, BBE-Boggs Engineering LLC, authorized agent, is
requesting approval of City Commission to reassign the future land use on .5+- acres of
land from MDR Medium Density Residential to RO Residential Office and to reassign the
zoning on .5+- acres of land from R-3 residential to PF Professional.
RECOMMENDATION
At a regular meeting on October 27, 2026, the Planning and Zoning Board made a
recommendation of approval to City Commission to reassign the future land use on .5+-
acres of land from MDR Medium Density Residential to RO Residential Office and to
reassign the zoning on .5+- acres of land from R-3 residential to PF Professional, finding
that proposed land use and zoning designations are consistent with the City’s
comprehensive plan and land development regulations.
BACKGROUND:
Current Future Land Use Designation: MDR Medium Density Residential
Current Zoning Designation: R-3 Residential
The .5-acre vacant parcel is located south of State Road 60 and adjacent to the existing
MidFlorida Credit Union. In January 2022, Ordinance No. 2021-28 was adopted, vacating
a portion of the Gibson Avenue right-of-way that traversed the subject property.
Page 1 of 3

MidFlorida Credit Union is proposing to construct a parking lot on the subject parcel to
provide additional parking for employees and accommodate overflow parking demand.
The parking will be for MidFlorida employees and customers only. An auto parking
establishment is permitted by right within the PF district.
Comprehensive Plan: RO Residential Office
Policy I.1.2.11
The primary function of the Residential-Office (RO) classification is to permit low intensity
office and commercial uses in conjunction with residential use.
Location criteria: This classification may serve as a transition between commercial and
residential use or as a buffer where a major highway intersects an existing residential
neighborhood. The RO classification is intended primarily for developed areas to provide
viable economic use and redevelopment option for residential areas that have declined
in value because of their location on major roadways.
Typical uses/intensity: Professional offices, medical facilities, institutional and educational
uses and public facilities. Single family dwellings, two- and three-family units, and multi-
family units up to 12 units per gross acre. Up to a maximum FAR of .25.
Development criteria: Mixed use developments are encouraged. Off-site residential uses
shall be buffered from non-residential uses. Access to major collector and arterial
roadways.
Zoning: PF Professional
Sec 23-401 District Classifications
This district is designed to permit a mix of offices, medical and light commercial
businesses and medium density residential uses on major roadways and to provide areas
for support businesses to major facilities such as hospitals.
Staff Findings:
1. The requested land use and zoning designations are consistent with the City’s
comprehensive plan and land development regulations.
2. An auto parking establishment is permitted within the PF Professional zoning
district.
CODE REFERENCES  AND REVIEW CRITERIA
The City Commission assigns future Land Use and Zoning designations by ordinances
upon a recommendation from the Planning & Zoning Board. The adopted land use
amendment ordinance is transmitted to the state’s Department of Economic Opportunity
(DEO) for review. The DEO reviews the land use designations as appropriate with
reference to the City’s Comprehensive Plan.
Page 2 of 3

The total acreage will be transmitted to DEO as a small-scale Future Land Use Map
Amendment.
OTHER OPTIONS
Decline to reassign the future land use and zoning.
FISCAL IMPACT
None.
ATTACHMENTS
Ordinance 2026-35
Ordinance 2026-36
List of noticed property owners
Page 3 of 3

ORDINANCE 2026-35
AN ORDINANCE  OF  THE CITY OF LAKE WALES,  POLK COUNTY,  FLORIDA,
AMENDING  THE FUTURE LAND USE MAP  OF THE COMPREHENSIVE  PLAN TO
CHANGE  LAND USE DESIGNATIONS ON APPROXIMATELY  .5+/- ACRE OF LAND
SOUTH OF STATE ROAD  60, FROM CITY OF LAKE WALES DESIGNATION MDR
MEDIUM DENSITY RESIDENTIAL, TO CITY OF LAKE WALES DESIGNATION RO
RESIDENTIAL OFFICE; PROVIDING FOR SEVERABILITY; AND PROVIDING FOR
AN EFFECTIVE DATE.
WHEREAS, the request implements requirements contained in Part II, Chapter 163 of
Florida Statutes, and therefore is exempt from requiring a Business Impact Estimate as
set forth by Chapter 2023-309, Laws of Fla. (CS/CS/SB 170 (2023).
BE IT ENACTED by the City Commission of the City of Lake Wales, Polk County, Florida:
SECTION 1 The Future Land Use Map (FLUM) of the Comprehensive Plan is hereby
amended to change the land use designation on approximately .5+/- acre
of	property,	as	follows:

SECTION 2 The applicability and effect of the Lake Wales Comprehensive Plan, as
amended, shall be as provided by the Local Government Planning and Land
Development Regulation Act, Section 163.3215, Florida Statutes, and this
ordinance.
SECTION 3 If any clause, section or provision of this ordinance or any fee imposed
pursuant to this ordinance shall be declared unconstitutional or invalid for
any reason or cause, the remaining portion of said ordinance or remaining
fees shall be in full force and effect and be valid as if such invalid portion
thereof had not been incorporated herein.
SECTION 4 Certified copies of the enacting ordinance, the City of Lake Wales
Comprehensive Plan and any amendment thereto, and the Lake Wales
Code of Ordinances shall be located in the Office of the City Clerk of Lake
Wales. The City Clerk shall also make copies available to the public for a
reasonable publication charge.
SECTION 5 This amendment shall not become effective until the state land planning
agency or the Administrative Commission, respectively, issues a final order
determining the amendment is in compliance.
