MEMORANDUM
DATE:	November 17, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commissioners
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
FROM:	Autumn Cochella, AICP, Growth Management Director
Shannon Hancock, Development Coordinator
SUBJECT:  Ordinance 2026-35 Future Land Use Map Amendment to assign .5+/- acres
RO Residential Office – 1st Reading
Public Hearing Required – Notice Requirements have been met
Ordinance 2026-36 Zoning Map Amendment to assign .5+- acres of land
PF Professional – 1st Reading
Public Hearing Required – Notice Requirements have been met
SYNOPSIS: Catherine Coyle, BBE-Boggs Engineering LLC, authorized agent, is
requesting approval of City Commission to reassign the future land use on .5+- acres of
land from MDR Medium Density Residential to RO Residential Office and to reassign the
zoning on .5+- acres of land from R-3 residential to PF Professional.
RECOMMENDATION
At a regular meeting on October 27, 2026, the Planning and Zoning Board made a
recommendation of approval to City Commission to reassign the future land use on .5+-
acres of land from MDR Medium Density Residential to RO Residential Office and to
reassign the zoning on .5+- acres of land from R-3 residential to PF Professional, finding
that proposed land use and zoning designations are consistent with the City’s
comprehensive plan and land development regulations.
BACKGROUND:
Current Future Land Use Designation: MDR Medium Density Residential
Current Zoning Designation: R-3 Residential
The .5-acre vacant parcel is located south of State Road 60 and adjacent to the existing
MidFlorida Credit Union. In January 2022, Ordinance No. 2021-28 was adopted, vacating
a portion of the Gibson Avenue right-of-way that traversed the subject property.
Page 1 of 3

MidFlorida Credit Union is proposing to construct a parking lot on the subject parcel to
provide additional parking for employees and accommodate overflow parking demand.
The parking will be for MidFlorida employees and customers only. An auto parking
establishment is permitted by right within the PF district.
Comprehensive Plan: RO Residential Office
Policy I.1.2.11
The primary function of the Residential-Office (RO) classification is to permit low intensity
office and commercial uses in conjunction with residential use.
Location criteria: This classification may serve as a transition between commercial and
residential use or as a buffer where a major highway intersects an existing residential
neighborhood. The RO classification is intended primarily for developed areas to provide
viable economic use and redevelopment option for residential areas that have declined
in value because of their location on major roadways.
Typical uses/intensity: Professional offices, medical facilities, institutional and educational
uses and public facilities. Single family dwellings, two- and three-family units, and multi-
family units up to 12 units per gross acre. Up to a maximum FAR of .25.
Development criteria: Mixed use developments are encouraged. Off-site residential uses
shall be buffered from non-residential uses. Access to major collector and arterial
roadways.
Zoning: PF Professional
Sec 23-401 District Classifications
This district is designed to permit a mix of offices, medical and light commercial
businesses and medium density residential uses on major roadways and to provide areas
for support businesses to major facilities such as hospitals.
Staff Findings:
1. The requested land use and zoning designations are consistent with the City’s
comprehensive plan and land development regulations.
2. An auto parking establishment is permitted within the PF Professional zoning
district.
CODE REFERENCES  AND REVIEW CRITERIA
The City Commission assigns future Land Use and Zoning designations by ordinances
upon a recommendation from the Planning & Zoning Board. The adopted land use
amendment ordinance is transmitted to the state’s Department of Economic Opportunity
(DEO) for review. The DEO reviews the land use designations as appropriate with
reference to the City’s Comprehensive Plan.
Page 2 of 3

The total acreage will be transmitted to DEO as a small-scale Future Land Use Map
Amendment.
OTHER OPTIONS
Decline to reassign the future land use and zoning.
FISCAL IMPACT
None.

SECTION 2 If any clause, section or provision of this ordinance or any fee imposed
pursuant to this ordinance shall be declared unconstitutional or invalid for
any reason or cause, the remaining portion of said ordinance or remaining
fees shall be in full force and effect and be valid as if such invalid portion
thereof had not been incorporated herein.
SECTION 3 Certified copies of the enacting ordinance, the City of Lake Wales
Comprehensive Plan and any amendment thereto, and the Lake Wales
Code of Ordinances shall be located in the Office of the City Clerk of Lake
Wales. The City Clerk shall also make copies available to the public for a
reasonable publication charge.
SECTION 4 This amendment shall not become effective until the state land planning
agency or the Administrative Commission, respectively, issues a final order
determining the Future Land Use amendment is in compliance.

and was adopted on Second Reading after Public Hearing on  , 2026.

MEMORANDUM
DATE:	November 17, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commissioners
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
FROM:	Autumn Cochella, AICP, Growth Management Director
SUBJECT:  Ordinance 2026-41 Amendments to Chapter 23 Land Development
Regulations
PUBLIC HEARING required
SYNOPSIS: Request to amend certain sections of the Land Development Regulations.
RECOMMENDATION
At a regular meeting on October 27, 2026, the Planning and Zoning Board made a
recommendation of approval to City Commission to amend the following sections of
Chapter 23 Land Development Regulations:
  Table 23-421 Permitted Uses
  Section 23-401 District classifications
BACKGROUND
Permitted Use Chart
1.) Allow Personal Service within the BP Business Park as a Permitted use (P).
A unit within the Longleaf Business Park on Highway 27 is being considered for a
gym (Health Club by code) by a building owner and tenant. The Business Park has
been considerably inactive over its lifetime. Once intended as an employment hub
for light industrial and professional uses, it now consists of a mix of uses, including
training and education, assembly and light manufacturing, café, hotel, professional
offices, and some direct to consumer uses.
With the recent sale of property within the park to a hotel group, a mix of support
uses is becoming more appropriate. The City is supportive of the proposed new
Health Club use and finds that it would be a beneficial addition to the south end of
the city. Per city code, a personal service uses are those which provide for the
needs for both individual customers, as well as businesses.
1 | P age

Pursuant to Section 23-802 Definitions, a Health Club is considered a personal
service. Other personal services are described as, an establishment which
provides for the needs of customers, whether individuals or businesses, and their
possessions. Personal service establishments include beauty and barber shops,
dry cleaning pick-up services, printing shops, frame shops, photo shops, health
clubs, spas, and appliance repair shops, but not laundromats.
Accompanying this change is a request to amend the way the code describes the
BP zoning classification so to better reflect the current interests and activities
within the BP designated areas.
Policy I.1.2.7. Business Park Center of the comprehensive plan, the
corresponding future land use designation, allows for up to 25% of the BP area
to be utilized for support commercial activities.
2.) Allow Indoor Amusement within PF Professional zoning districts as a Special
Exception Use Permit.
The owner of a building on Scenic Highway has asked for consideration to allow
an event space in their building that is for sale. The City does not have a specific
use category for events and assembly; however, the use Amusement
Establishment, Indoor has historically been used, as it caters to the gathering of
people and needs consideration for sufficient parking. The particular building was
used as a funeral home for many years, and is well-suited to be re-established as
a gathering space for events.
Keeping the use as S – Special Permit, rather than P – Permitted, allows the Board
to consider these types of uses on a site-by-site basis to ensure the use is
compatible with the area directly surrounding the specific property, and that the
site can handle the parking needs and safe site circulation.
3.) Amend the description of the BP – Business Park zoning district classification
BP Business park. This district is intended for discrete areas established and
designed for a mixture of professional, commercial enterprises, offices, service
businesses, light industrial, wholesale, and other professional uses grouped
together. including hotels and motels and car dealerships, and excluding retail,
drive-up restaurants, service and other commercial uses catering directly to
consumers except those accessory to a principal use.
2 | P age

CODE REFERENCES
Sec. 23-401 District Classifications
Table 23-421 Permitted Uses
Section 23-802 Definitions
FISCAL IMPACT
None.
ATTACHMENTS
Ordinance 2026-41
3 | P age

ORDINANCE 2026-41
AN ORDINANCE	OF THE  CITY OF LAKE  WALES, POLK  COUNTY,  FLORIDA,
AMENDING  THE CODE OF ORDINANCES  CHAPTER  23, ZONING, LAND USE AND
DEVELOPMENT	REGULATIONS;	AMENDING	SECTION	23-401  DISTRICT
CLASSIFICATIONS, AND AMENDING TABLE 23-421 PERMITTED USES; PROVIDING
FOR SEVERABILITY; AND PROVIDING FOR AN EFFECTIVE DATE.
WHEREAS, at a regular meeting on October 27, 2026, the City’s Planning and
Zoning Board made a recommendation of approval of proposed changes to the Land
Development Code; and,
WHEREAS, the City of Lake Wales Planning and Zoning Board is designated as
the Local Planning Agency pursuant to the Local Government Comprehensive Planning
and Land Development Regulation Act, F.S. ch. 163, Part II, (hereafter “Planning Board”)
as set forth in Section 23-205, Code; and,
WHEREAS, a public hearing was advertised and noticed in accordance with state
law before the Planning Board, and the Planning Board, after due consideration of public
testimony and other competent, substantial evidence, has recommended to the City
Commission the modifications set forth in this ordinance; and,
WHEREAS, the City Commission after due public notice advertised and held two
public hearings as required by Section 166.041, Florida Statutes; and,
WHEREAS,  the City Commission has received public input and testimony, the
recommendation of its Planning Board, and other competent, substantial evidence, and
based thereon finds that the amendments are reasonable and will not affect the public
interest; and,
WHEREAS, the request implements requirements contained in Part II, Chapter
163 of Florida Statutes, and therefore is exempt from requiring a Business Impact
Estimate as set forth by Chapter 2023-309, Laws of Fla. (CS/CS/SB 170 (2023).
NOW THEREFORE, BE IT ENACTED by the City Commission of the City of Lake
Wales, Florida, as follows:
SECTION 1: FINDINGS. The City Commission hereby adopts and incorporates the
above stated Recitals as legislative findings that support and form the basis for the
adoption of this ordinance.

SECTION 2: AMENDMENTS. Chapter 23, Zoning, Development, and Land Use
Regulations of the Code of Ordinances of the City of Lake Wales, Florida (hereafter
“Code”), is hereby amended to read as follows:
23-401 District Classifications
b. Professional, commercial and industrial districts.
PF Professional district. This district is designed to permit a mix of offices, medical and
light commercial businesses and medium density residential uses on major roadways
and to provide areas for support businesses to major facilities such as hospitals.
C-1 Downtown commercial district. This district is designed to encourage and facilitate
a mix of residential uses and commercial activities in compact, pedestrian oriented,
central business districts with a variety of traditional downtown uses such as small
retail stores, offices, restaurants, hotels, and apartments.
C-1A Downtown core district. This sub-district of the C-1 district is designed to create
a mixed-use center for day-time and evening cultural and commercial activities where
people live, work, or visit and where they can enjoy historic architecture, shopping,
dining, music, special events, strolling, and relaxing in public spaces.
C-2 Commercial. This district is designed to encourage medium-scale commercial
uses, primarily in groupings or on inter-connected sites rather than on isolated
properties.
C-2R Commercial/residential district. This district is designed to encourage a mix of
residential and light commercial uses in groupings and on individual lots on the
periphery of the central business districts.
C-3 Highway commercial district. This district is designed to permit the development of
commercial areas in groupings on major highways and to allow medium and large-
scale commercial establishments and a full range of commercial and professional
uses.
C-4 Neighborhood commercial district. This district is designed to permit the
development of local commercial areas to serve surrounding residential areas with
small-scale convenience goods and personal services.
C-5 Village center district. This district is designed to allow the development of new
commercial centers, planned within large residential developments to provide a wide
range of small to medium sized commercial uses and personal services in a setting
geared to both pedestrian and vehicular traffic.
LCI Limited commercial-industrial district. This district is designed to permit a mix of
commercial and industrial uses.

BP Business park. This district is intended for discrete areas established and designed
for a mixture of professional, commercial enterprises, offices, service businesses, light
industrial, wholesale, and other professional uses, grouped together. including hotels
and motels and car dealerships, and excluding retail, drive-up restaurants, service and
other commercial uses catering directly to consumers except those accessory to a
principal use.
I-1 Industrial district, general. This district is intended to provide for the development of
general manufacturing, fabrication, processing, assembly, warehousing & distribution,
and wholesale activities industrial uses, primarily on vacant lands and in industrial
park settings. General commercial, office, and research/development uses necessary
to support the industrial area are also permitted.
I-2 Industrial infill district. This district is intended to facilitate infill and redevelopment
of industrial uses in developed industrial areas and to permit the development of
manufacturing and industrial uses on vacant property in largely developed areas.
PDMU Planned Development Mixed-use district. This district is intended to implement
Policy 2.18 of the Comprehensive Plan, in order to facilitate large-scale, mixed-use
master planned development on large tracts of land. Properties zoned PDMU shall
seek approval of a master development plan, in accordance with division 5, section
23-450 of these land use and development regulations.
D-MU Downtown Mixed-use district. This district is designed to encourage and
facilitate a mix of residential uses and commercial activities in a compact, traditional
downtown setting including retail shopfronts, offices, restaurants, hotels, apartments,
live/work units and rowhouses in street-oriented buildings that line walkable,
pedestrian-oriented streets.

MEMORANDUM
DATE:	November 17, 2026
TO:	Honorable Mayor and City Commissioners
VIA:	James Slaton, City Manager
FROM:	Autumn Cochella, AICP, Growth Management Director
SUBJECT:  Oakwood Ridge
1.) Ordinance 2026-32 - Annexation
AN ORDINANCE OF THE CITY OF LAKE WALES, POLK COUNTY, FLORIDA
PROVIDING FOR THE ANNEXATION OF APPROXIMATELY 146+/- ACRES OF

SYNOPSIS: Helmut Wyzisk III, President of Signature H Property Group and authorized
agent for owners, FLOWCC, LLC and VZCREATE, LLC, requests approval of City
Commission for annexation, to amend the Future Land Use Map and Zoning Map, and a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat to allow 558 single-family homes, and 5.195 acres of support
neighborhood commercial on 393+/- acres of land.
Page 2 of 10

RECOMMENDATION
At a regular meeting on October 27, 2026, the Planning and Zoning Board made a
recommendation of approval to City Commission for the following:
A Large-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 146+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of LDR Low Density Residential and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 393+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of R-1C Residential; a
Small-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of NAC Neighborhood Activity Center and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of C-4 Neighborhood
Commercial; and the approval of Oakwood Ridge, a 393+/- acre Preliminary Subdivision
Plat, contingent upon the adoption of Annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City
Commission, and with conditions of approval, finding that the proposed Future Land
Use and Zoning Map designations are compatible with surrounding area, and are
consistent with the City’s Comprehensive Plan and Land Use Regulations, and that the
proposed Preliminary Subdivision Plat is designed to the City’s Land Development
Regulations, and with the following condition:
1.) The applicant shall continue coordination with the existing Oakwood community,
as well as Growth Management Staff, to resolve any outstanding issues related
to tree preservation and school concurrency prior to the City Commission
hearing.
BACKGROUND
The subject 392.53 acres of land is the former Oakwood Golf Club located west of
Capps Road, and north of the Lake Wales Country Club along State Road 60 East.
Approximately 242 acres of the project area are already within the Lake Wales city limits
with a land use designation of Low Density Residential, while the balance of the project
area is within unincorporated Polk County and is petitioned for annexation into the city.
Accompanying the requests for land use and zoning, the applicant has prepared a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat designed to both meet and exceed the standards of the
requested zoning designation of R-1C residential, and includes a small support non-
residential component.
Proposed Development:
  Project Area: 393 acres
  Number of dwelling units: 558
143 65-foot-wide lots

75 alley-loaded townhomes
o
  Three commercial outparcels
  193.4 acres of open space (49.2% of the site)
  27 acres of recreation area provided, including trails and amenities, where 2.11
acres is required by code

  No waivers of strict compliance are requested
LAKE WALES ENVISIONED
Economy E3 Provide a variety of housing to attract employees and industry
Staff Comment: A wide variety of housing options are proposed, including alley-loaded
townhomes, and lots consistent with those within the surrounding area. Through their
community outreach efforts, the applicant received feedback that a need for executive
style housing exists in the city. This resulted in the inclusion of 53 executive lots within
the project.
Economy E4 Concentrate on employee quality of life; promote Lake Wales for remote
workers
Staff Comment: Neighborhoods that include ample trails and recreation opportunities, and
commercial conveniences close to home, may be attractive to remote workers who can
utilize nearby neighborhood amenities without automobile reliance.
Comprehensive Plan: Applicable Objectives and Policies
Policy I.1.2.12 Low Density Residential (LDR) The primary function of the Low Density
Residential (LDR) classification is to provide areas for housing, primarily single-family,
with an overall density of 5 or fewer dwelling units per acre. The LDR classification is
intended to preserve the character of existing single family residential areas with large
lots and to allow the continued development of these areas. It is also intended for
classification of vacant parcels, particularly newly annexed lands on the periphery of the
City.
Location criteria: LDR is appropriate for vacant lands a half mile or more from arterial
roadways and CACs-Community Activity Centers and RACs-Regional Activity Centers.
Existing residential areas with a density of 3 or fewer units per acre on average should
also be assigned the LDR designation.
Typical uses: Housing up to 5 units per acre. Single-family houses. Two- and three-family
units, multi-family units may be allowed depending on compatibility with adjoining
residential areas, the development suitability of the site, and the availability of public
facilities and services. Uses that are customarily found in residential neighborhoods and
compatible with residential development, such as religious, educational, public facility
uses, and assisted living facilities with a maximum FAR of .25. Clustering of housing units
is encouraged to preserve open space and natural resources.
Policy I.1.2.6. Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) The primary function of the
Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) classification is to accommodate businesses and
services oriented to the needs of residents in the surrounding neighborhoods. The NACs
Page 4 of 10

are also intended as village centers in residential developments. They should be designed
as community centers and integral parts of residential neighborhoods, with businesses
located in groups rather than in a strip along a roadway and should be accessible via local
roads and pedestrian/bike paths from nearby residential areas.
Typical uses/intensity: Convenience store, drug store, bakery, florist, professional offices
and personal services. 20,000 - 90,000 SQ.FT. GLA.
Maximum FAR = .25 except in village centers where the maximum FAR shall be 2.0.
Residential uses shall constitute a maximum of 40% of a contiguous NAC district and
shall not exceed 12 units per acre. Location criteria: Accessible to surrounding residential
neighborhoods. Where City water and sanitary sewer are available. Direct access to a
collector or arterial road. A minimum of a mile from another NAC or another activity center
(CAC or RAC). Maximum size 20 acres, except that a mixed-use NAC designed as a
village center in a residential development may encompass up to 50 acres. Development
criteria: • NACs shall be a planned grouping of businesses with a common access point
rather than a strip commercial area; • Access to an NAC shall be from an arterial road or
from a collector road connecting residential areas to a larger roadway. • Pedestrian/bike
paths shall be required to connect the NAC to the surrounding neighborhoods it serves.
• Access to NAC businesses shall be designed primarily for accessibility by the pedestrian
and bicycle rather than by the motorized vehicle. Drive-up banks and restaurants shall
require a special permit. • New village centers shall be designed to mimic traditional
central business districts, with a mix of residential and non-residential uses and
pedestrian oriented layouts.
Objective I.1.5. Desired Urban Growth Pattern A compact, efficient, and sustainable
pattern of development that provides convenient access for residents to urban services
and amenities, avoids urban sprawl, strip development and leap-frog development,
protects natural and historic resources, is compatible with existing land uses and is
coordinated with and consistent with the adopted growth management plans of Polk
County, adjacent municipalities, and with applicable regional and state agency plans and
regulations. Policy I.1.5.1 FLUM - The Future Land Use Map is the key instrument for
achieving Objective I.1.5. The distribution of land use designations on the FLUM shall be
carefully chosen and maintained to promote the desired growth pattern as expressed in
this comprehensive plan, particularly under this objective (Objective I.1.5 – Desired Urban
Growth Pattern) and Objective I.1.2 – Future Land Use Classification System. Locate
future land uses on the Future Land Use Map at densities and intensities that will
discourage urban sprawl and leap-frog development that unduly depletes the physical,
social, and fiscal resources of the city.
Page 5 of 10

Policy I.1.5.2. Functional mix of land uses - The land development regulations shall
promote an integrated mix of residential and non-residential land uses consistent with the
future land use classification system of this element and shall include mechanisms to
promote innovative site design.
Policy I.1.5.5. NACs-Neighborhood Activity Centers shall be designated for convenience
shopping to serve residential areas at a distance from commercial nodes.
Policy I.1.5.12 The land development regulations shall require continued coordination
with Polk County and the Florida Department of Transportation to mitigate the traffic
impacts of development adjacent to Highway 27 and S.R. 60 in the Lake Wales Planning
Area and with agencies as appropriate on access management, facility design options,
right-of-way needs, and on-site traffic flow.
Policy I.1.9.2. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to use, maintain,
development, and improve his or her property for personal use or the use of any other
person, subject to state law and local ordinances.
Policy I.1.9.4. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to dispose of his or
her property through sale or gift.
Zoning: Sec 23-401 District Classifications
R-1C Residential - This district is designed primarily to permit the continued
development of already platted medium density single-family residential areas. The R-
1C zoning designation is intended for assignment to lands classified as MDR - Medium
Density Residential and may be assigned to lands classified as HDR - High Density
Residential on the future land use map of the Comprehensive Plan.
Dimensional and area standards:
Single-family detached:
Minimum lot size: 8,000SF
Minimum street frontage: 50SF
Minimum lot width: 65’
Minimum floor area of home: 1,200SF
Minimum front setback: 0’
Minimum side setback: 10’
Minimum rear setback: 15’
Maximum lot coverage: 60%
Maximum building height: 35’
Single-family detached (townhomes)
Minimum lot size: 2,700SF
Minimum lot width: 25’
Page 6 of 10

Minimum floor area of home: 1,200SF
Minimum front setback: 0’
Minimum exterior side setback: 15’
Minimum interior side setback: 0’
Minimum rear setback: 15’
Maximum lot coverage: 50%
Maximum building height: 35’
Staff Comment: Although the Low-Density Residential land use classification typically
corresponds to the R-1A and R-1B zoning designations, the project proposes a range
of lot sizes, the majority of which exceed the minimum lot dimensions of the R-1A and
R-1B requirements, and the smallest lots exceeding the minimum lot square-footage for
R-1C. Lots within the existing Oakwood neighborhood range from 56’ to 100+’ in width,
while the adjacent Lake Wales Country Club contains a mix of lot sizes and housing
types, such as 100’-wide single-family detached lots, and 50’-wide single-family
attached homes. Additionally, due to the amount of preservation space and open space
proposed, the overall density of the site remains below the low-density threshold, at
1.42 units per gross acre.
C-4 Neighborhood Commercial – This district is designed to permit the development
of local commercial areas to serve surrounding residential areas with small-scale
convenience goods and personal services.
Staff Comment: 5.195 acres of non-residential support uses are proposed within the
northwest area. At such time that these three lots are proposed for development, a
determination will be made by the Administrative Official pursuant to Sec. 23-222.3.b.
determination of major and minor projects, as well as Table 23-421 Permitted Uses, as
to the process for review and approval. The C-4 zoning district permits support
commercial uses such as cultural facilities, daycares, cafes, professional offices, and
personal service.
IMPACT ANALYSIS (provided by applicant per 23-704.2 Burden of proof. The
applicant shall bear the burden of showing compliance with the adopted levels of
service and meeting the concurrency test).
Sec. 23-708 A certificate of concurrency will not be granted for a proposed
development unless the city finds that adequate capacity for concurrency facilities
exists at or above the adopted level of service in order to accommodate the impacts of
the proposed development, or that improvements necessary to bring concurrency
facilities up to their adopted level of service will be in place concurrent with the
impacts of the development.

Staff Comments:
1.) 10.3 Projected Demand and Capacity – Statement from the City’s Public Work’s
Director: While capacity may be available, this does not constitute confirmation
or reservation of utility capacity for the proposed development. Final confirmation
will be subject to further review, verification of available system capacity, and the
City's applicable utility requirements.
2.) 13. Trip Generation (Traffic): See Attachment C.
Access to the site is obtained from Burns Avenue (County Road 17A), and
Capps Road, both County Roads. City Planning Staff reached out to the Polk
County Roads and Drainage coordinator on September 9th to give them an
overview of the project and attempt early coordination.
Following the Planning Board Review meeting where the project was discussed
on September 8, the applicant made a requested revision to the plan to include
a future stub out connection to the existing sand mine operation, in anticipation
to the future development of that site once mining ceases. An emergency
services road connection was also created at the southwest portion of the site
to the corner of Sunset Drive and Evergreen Drive.
Conditions of Approval:
1.) A Detailed Landscape Plan, including a tree preservation plan in partnership with
Lake Wales Heritage, is required at Site Development Permit Submittal and will be
reviewed and approved by Staff and the City’s Horticulturist.
2.) Decorative, dark sky friendly street lighting to minimize glare and reduce light
pollution. Street lighting design to be approved by the City, and maintained by the
HOA.
3.) Approval of the PSP does not secure utility capacity or guarantee concurrency.
The developer must enter into a developer’s agreement, capacity queue
agreement and/or utilities agreement with the City, as applicable, and at the
administrative official’s discretion. A utility agreement shall include, but is not
limited to, the transfer of wells if any exist on site.
4.) Although the City does not have front setback requirements, all corner lots shall
have a minimum front setback of 15 feet from the property line within the functional
side yard.
5.) Although access is not needed from an FDOT roadway, the City encourages
coordination with FDOT when future improvements connect to an FDOT Facility
(intersection of Capps Road and State Road 60).
6.) Approval of the map amendments shall be contingent upon the adoption of
annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City Commission.
7.) Approval the PSP shall be contingent upon the adoption of the annexation, future
land use, and zoning ordinances by City Commission.
Page 8 of 10

STAFF FINDINGS:
1. This development site is located within the City’s utility service area and will
maximize existing infrastructure investments by connecting to municipal water and
sewer. According to the City’s Chapter 180 Utility Service Area, the City has a duty
to serve the project with water and sewer.
2. The PSP is designed to meet and/or exceed all requirements of the Land
Development Regulations, including the dimensional and area standards
according to the requested zoning designations, and no waivers of strict
compliance are requested.
3. The recommended Future Land Use categories are generally consistent with the
Future Land Use Element of the Comprehensive Plan.
4. The recommended Zoning Designations are generally consistent with Section 23-
401 of the Land Development Regulations.
5. The neighborhood design is compatible with the existing neighborhoods within the
surrounding area.
6. At a Development Review Committee meeting on August 6, 2026, the Committee
was generally favorable of the proposed development, finding that it was a suitable
redevelopment of the defunct golf course, and that the annexation of the additional
land would improve the form of the city boundary.
7. The applicant has satisfied the Planning and Zoning Board’s additional conditions
of approval by completing the following:
a.) The applicant has partnered with Lake Wales Heritage in order to design an
Olmstead-inspired tree preservation plan (see attachment: Tree Preservation
Statement).
b.) The applicant has conducted meetings with individual property owners within
the existing Oakwood community in order to address specific landscape
buffering and tree preservation opportunities.
c.) The applicant has revised the Development Impact Data to correctly identify
Polk County Public Schools for school concurrency and to better address Level
of Service standards pursuant to Section 23-706 of city code.
d.) The 65-foot-wide lots abutting Country Oaks have been increased in size to 80-
foot-wide lots in order to increase compatibility with the existing home lots.
FISCAL IMPACT
The project may generate $5.2 million in City impact fees.
OTHER OPTIONS
Decline to annex the property.

SAID LANDS SITUATE LYING AND BEING IN POLK COUNTY, FLORIDA.
SECTION 2 All of the public property, lots, easements, streets, roads, and public right-
of-way, now located and dedicated, acquired, platted or conveyed to the
public in the territory described in Section 1, shall be transferred to the City
of Lake Wales without consideration for the same uses as originally
conveyed.
SECTION 3 All ordinances of the City of Lake Wales and all laws heretofore passed by
the legislature of the State of Florida, relating to and which now or hereafter
constitute its charter, shall apply to and have the same force and effect in
all of the territory described in Section 1 of this ordinance as if all of such
territory had been part of the City of Lake Wales at the time of the passage
and approval of such laws and ordinances.
SECTION 4 All of the area to be annexed shall be entitled to the same rights and benefits
as those, which exist in the City of Lake Wales upon the effective date of
annexation.
SECTION 5 If any portion or portions of the ordinance shall be declared to be invalid,
the remaining portion shall have the same force and effect, as though, such
invalid portion or portions had not been included.
SECTION 6 This ordinance shall become effective, and the territory shall be considered
annexed, immediately upon passage by the City Commission.
CERTIFIED AS TO PASSAGE this   day of   2026.
 
Mayor/Commissioner, City of Lake Wales

SYNOPSIS: Helmut Wyzisk III, President of Signature H Property Group and authorized
agent for owners, FLOWCC, LLC and VZCREATE, LLC, requests approval of City
Commission for annexation, to amend the Future Land Use Map and Zoning Map, and a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat to allow 558 single-family homes, and 5.195 acres of support
neighborhood commercial on 393+/- acres of land.
Page 2 of 10

RECOMMENDATION
At a regular meeting on October 27, 2026, the Planning and Zoning Board made a
recommendation of approval to City Commission for the following:
A Large-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 146+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of LDR Low Density Residential and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 393+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of R-1C Residential; a
Small-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of NAC Neighborhood Activity Center and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of C-4 Neighborhood
Commercial; and the approval of Oakwood Ridge, a 393+/- acre Preliminary Subdivision
Plat, contingent upon the adoption of Annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City
Commission, and with conditions of approval, finding that the proposed Future Land
Use and Zoning Map designations are compatible with surrounding area, and are
consistent with the City’s Comprehensive Plan and Land Use Regulations, and that the
proposed Preliminary Subdivision Plat is designed to the City’s Land Development
Regulations, and with the following condition:
1.) The applicant shall continue coordination with the existing Oakwood community,
as well as Growth Management Staff, to resolve any outstanding issues related
to tree preservation and school concurrency prior to the City Commission
hearing.
BACKGROUND
The subject 392.53 acres of land is the former Oakwood Golf Club located west of
Capps Road, and north of the Lake Wales Country Club along State Road 60 East.
Approximately 242 acres of the project area are already within the Lake Wales city limits
with a land use designation of Low Density Residential, while the balance of the project
area is within unincorporated Polk County and is petitioned for annexation into the city.
Accompanying the requests for land use and zoning, the applicant has prepared a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat designed to both meet and exceed the standards of the
requested zoning designation of R-1C residential, and includes a small support non-
residential component.
Proposed Development:
  Project Area: 393 acres
  Number of dwelling units: 558
143 65-foot-wide lots
o
287 80-foot-wide lots
o
53 120-foot-wide lots
o
75 alley-loaded townhomes
o
  Three commercial outparcels
  193.4 acres of open space (49.2% of the site)
  27 acres of recreation area provided, including trails and amenities, where 2.11
acres is required by code
Page 3 of 10

  No waivers of strict compliance are requested
LAKE WALES ENVISIONED
Economy E3 Provide a variety of housing to attract employees and industry
Staff Comment: A wide variety of housing options are proposed, including alley-loaded
townhomes, and lots consistent with those within the surrounding area. Through their
community outreach efforts, the applicant received feedback that a need for executive
style housing exists in the city. This resulted in the inclusion of 53 executive lots within
the project.
Economy E4 Concentrate on employee quality of life; promote Lake Wales for remote
workers
Staff Comment: Neighborhoods that include ample trails and recreation opportunities, and
commercial conveniences close to home, may be attractive to remote workers who can
utilize nearby neighborhood amenities without automobile reliance.
Comprehensive Plan: Applicable Objectives and Policies
Policy I.1.2.12 Low Density Residential (LDR) The primary function of the Low Density
Residential (LDR) classification is to provide areas for housing, primarily single-family,
with an overall density of 5 or fewer dwelling units per acre. The LDR classification is
intended to preserve the character of existing single family residential areas with large
lots and to allow the continued development of these areas. It is also intended for
classification of vacant parcels, particularly newly annexed lands on the periphery of the
City.
Location criteria: LDR is appropriate for vacant lands a half mile or more from arterial
roadways and CACs-Community Activity Centers and RACs-Regional Activity Centers.
Existing residential areas with a density of 3 or fewer units per acre on average should
also be assigned the LDR designation.
Typical uses: Housing up to 5 units per acre. Single-family houses. Two- and three-family
units, multi-family units may be allowed depending on compatibility with adjoining
residential areas, the development suitability of the site, and the availability of public
facilities and services. Uses that are customarily found in residential neighborhoods and
compatible with residential development, such as religious, educational, public facility
uses, and assisted living facilities with a maximum FAR of .25. Clustering of housing units
is encouraged to preserve open space and natural resources.
Policy I.1.2.6. Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) The primary function of the
Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) classification is to accommodate businesses and
services oriented to the needs of residents in the surrounding neighborhoods. The NACs
Page 4 of 10

are also intended as village centers in residential developments. They should be designed
as community centers and integral parts of residential neighborhoods, with businesses
located in groups rather than in a strip along a roadway and should be accessible via local
roads and pedestrian/bike paths from nearby residential areas.
Typical uses/intensity: Convenience store, drug store, bakery, florist, professional offices
and personal services. 20,000 - 90,000 SQ.FT. GLA.
Maximum FAR = .25 except in village centers where the maximum FAR shall be 2.0.
Residential uses shall constitute a maximum of 40% of a contiguous NAC district and
shall not exceed 12 units per acre. Location criteria: Accessible to surrounding residential
neighborhoods. Where City water and sanitary sewer are available. Direct access to a
collector or arterial road. A minimum of a mile from another NAC or another activity center
(CAC or RAC). Maximum size 20 acres, except that a mixed-use NAC designed as a
village center in a residential development may encompass up to 50 acres. Development
criteria: • NACs shall be a planned grouping of businesses with a common access point
rather than a strip commercial area; • Access to an NAC shall be from an arterial road or
from a collector road connecting residential areas to a larger roadway. • Pedestrian/bike
paths shall be required to connect the NAC to the surrounding neighborhoods it serves.
• Access to NAC businesses shall be designed primarily for accessibility by the pedestrian
and bicycle rather than by the motorized vehicle. Drive-up banks and restaurants shall
require a special permit. • New village centers shall be designed to mimic traditional
central business districts, with a mix of residential and non-residential uses and
pedestrian oriented layouts.
Objective I.1.5. Desired Urban Growth Pattern A compact, efficient, and sustainable
pattern of development that provides convenient access for residents to urban services
and amenities, avoids urban sprawl, strip development and leap-frog development,
protects natural and historic resources, is compatible with existing land uses and is
coordinated with and consistent with the adopted growth management plans of Polk
County, adjacent municipalities, and with applicable regional and state agency plans and
regulations. Policy I.1.5.1 FLUM - The Future Land Use Map is the key instrument for
achieving Objective I.1.5. The distribution of land use designations on the FLUM shall be
carefully chosen and maintained to promote the desired growth pattern as expressed in
this comprehensive plan, particularly under this objective (Objective I.1.5 – Desired Urban
Growth Pattern) and Objective I.1.2 – Future Land Use Classification System. Locate
future land uses on the Future Land Use Map at densities and intensities that will
discourage urban sprawl and leap-frog development that unduly depletes the physical,
social, and fiscal resources of the city.
Page 5 of 10

Policy I.1.5.2. Functional mix of land uses - The land development regulations shall
promote an integrated mix of residential and non-residential land uses consistent with the
future land use classification system of this element and shall include mechanisms to
promote innovative site design.
Policy I.1.5.5. NACs-Neighborhood Activity Centers shall be designated for convenience
shopping to serve residential areas at a distance from commercial nodes.
Policy I.1.5.12 The land development regulations shall require continued coordination
with Polk County and the Florida Department of Transportation to mitigate the traffic
impacts of development adjacent to Highway 27 and S.R. 60 in the Lake Wales Planning
Area and with agencies as appropriate on access management, facility design options,
right-of-way needs, and on-site traffic flow.
Policy I.1.9.2. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to use, maintain,
development, and improve his or her property for personal use or the use of any other
person, subject to state law and local ordinances.
Policy I.1.9.4. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to dispose of his or
her property through sale or gift.
Zoning: Sec 23-401 District Classifications
R-1C Residential - This district is designed primarily to permit the continued
development of already platted medium density single-family residential areas. The R-
1C zoning designation is intended for assignment to lands classified as MDR - Medium
Density Residential and may be assigned to lands classified as HDR - High Density
Residential on the future land use map of the Comprehensive Plan.
Dimensional and area standards:
Single-family detached:
Minimum lot size: 8,000SF
Minimum street frontage: 50SF
Minimum lot width: 65’
Minimum floor area of home: 1,200SF
Minimum front setback: 0’
Minimum side setback: 10’
Minimum rear setback: 15’
Maximum lot coverage: 60%
Maximum building height: 35’
Single-family detached (townhomes)
Minimum lot size: 2,700SF
Minimum lot width: 25’
Page 6 of 10

Minimum floor area of home: 1,200SF
Minimum front setback: 0’
Minimum exterior side setback: 15’
Minimum interior side setback: 0’
Minimum rear setback: 15’
Maximum lot coverage: 50%
Maximum building height: 35’
Staff Comment: Although the Low-Density Residential land use classification typically
corresponds to the R-1A and R-1B zoning designations, the project proposes a range
of lot sizes, the majority of which exceed the minimum lot dimensions of the R-1A and
R-1B requirements, and the smallest lots exceeding the minimum lot square-footage for
R-1C. Lots within the existing Oakwood neighborhood range from 56’ to 100+’ in width,
while the adjacent Lake Wales Country Club contains a mix of lot sizes and housing
types, such as 100’-wide single-family detached lots, and 50’-wide single-family
attached homes. Additionally, due to the amount of preservation space and open space
proposed, the overall density of the site remains below the low-density threshold, at
1.42 units per gross acre.
C-4 Neighborhood Commercial – This district is designed to permit the development
of local commercial areas to serve surrounding residential areas with small-scale
convenience goods and personal services.
Staff Comment: 5.195 acres of non-residential support uses are proposed within the
northwest area. At such time that these three lots are proposed for development, a
determination will be made by the Administrative Official pursuant to Sec. 23-222.3.b.
determination of major and minor projects, as well as Table 23-421 Permitted Uses, as
to the process for review and approval. The C-4 zoning district permits support
commercial uses such as cultural facilities, daycares, cafes, professional offices, and
personal service.
IMPACT ANALYSIS (provided by applicant per 23-704.2 Burden of proof. The
applicant shall bear the burden of showing compliance with the adopted levels of
service and meeting the concurrency test).
Sec. 23-708 A certificate of concurrency will not be granted for a proposed
development unless the city finds that adequate capacity for concurrency facilities
exists at or above the adopted level of service in order to accommodate the impacts of
the proposed development, or that improvements necessary to bring concurrency
facilities up to their adopted level of service will be in place concurrent with the
impacts of the development.
See Attachment B: Development Impact Analysis
Page 7 of 10

Staff Comments:
1.) 10.3 Projected Demand and Capacity – Statement from the City’s Public Work’s
Director: While capacity may be available, this does not constitute confirmation
or reservation of utility capacity for the proposed development. Final confirmation
will be subject to further review, verification of available system capacity, and the
City's applicable utility requirements.
2.) 13. Trip Generation (Traffic): See Attachment C.
Access to the site is obtained from Burns Avenue (County Road 17A), and
Capps Road, both County Roads. City Planning Staff reached out to the Polk
County Roads and Drainage coordinator on September 9th to give them an
overview of the project and attempt early coordination.
Following the Planning Board Review meeting where the project was discussed
on September 8, the applicant made a requested revision to the plan to include
a future stub out connection to the existing sand mine operation, in anticipation
to the future development of that site once mining ceases. An emergency
services road connection was also created at the southwest portion of the site
to the corner of Sunset Drive and Evergreen Drive.
Conditions of Approval:
1.) A Detailed Landscape Plan, including a tree preservation plan in partnership with
Lake Wales Heritage, is required at Site Development Permit Submittal and will be
reviewed and approved by Staff and the City’s Horticulturist.
2.) Decorative, dark sky friendly street lighting to minimize glare and reduce light
pollution. Street lighting design to be approved by the City, and maintained by the
HOA.
3.) Approval of the PSP does not secure utility capacity or guarantee concurrency.
The developer must enter into a developer’s agreement, capacity queue
agreement and/or utilities agreement with the City, as applicable, and at the
administrative official’s discretion. A utility agreement shall include, but is not
limited to, the transfer of wells if any exist on site.
4.) Although the City does not have front setback requirements, all corner lots shall
have a minimum front setback of 15 feet from the property line within the functional
side yard.
5.) Although access is not needed from an FDOT roadway, the City encourages
coordination with FDOT when future improvements connect to an FDOT Facility
(intersection of Capps Road and State Road 60).
6.) Approval of the map amendments shall be contingent upon the adoption of
annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City Commission.
7.) Approval the PSP shall be contingent upon the adoption of the annexation, future
land use, and zoning ordinances by City Commission.
Page 8 of 10

STAFF FINDINGS:
1. This development site is located within the City’s utility service area and will
maximize existing infrastructure investments by connecting to municipal water and
sewer. According to the City’s Chapter 180 Utility Service Area, the City has a duty
to serve the project with water and sewer.
2. The PSP is designed to meet and/or exceed all requirements of the Land
Development Regulations, including the dimensional and area standards
according to the requested zoning designations, and no waivers of strict
compliance are requested.
3. The recommended Future Land Use categories are generally consistent with the
Future Land Use Element of the Comprehensive Plan.
4. The recommended Zoning Designations are generally consistent with Section 23-
401 of the Land Development Regulations.
5. The neighborhood design is compatible with the existing neighborhoods within the
surrounding area.
6. At a Development Review Committee meeting on August 6, 2026, the Committee
was generally favorable of the proposed development, finding that it was a suitable
redevelopment of the defunct golf course, and that the annexation of the additional
land would improve the form of the city boundary.
7. The applicant has satisfied the Planning and Zoning Board’s additional conditions
of approval by completing the following:
a.) The applicant has partnered with Lake Wales Heritage in order to design an
Olmstead-inspired tree preservation plan (see attachment: Tree Preservation
Statement).
b.) The applicant has conducted meetings with individual property owners within
the existing Oakwood community in order to address specific landscape
buffering and tree preservation opportunities.
c.) The applicant has revised the Development Impact Data to correctly identify
Polk County Public Schools for school concurrency and to better address Level
of Service standards pursuant to Section 23-706 of city code.
d.) The 65-foot-wide lots abutting Country Oaks have been increased in size to 80-
foot-wide lots in order to increase compatibility with the existing home lots.
FISCAL IMPACT
The project may generate $5.2 million in City impact fees.
OTHER OPTIONS
Decline to annex the property.

SECTION 2 The applicability and effect of the Lake Wales Comprehensive Plan, as
amended, shall be as provided by the Local Government Planning and Land
Development Regulation Act, Section 163.3215, Florida Statutes, and this
ordinance.
SECTION 3 If any clause, section or provision of this ordinance or any fee imposed
pursuant to this ordinance shall be declared unconstitutional or invalid for
any reason or cause, the remaining portion of said ordinance or remaining
fees shall be in full force and effect and be valid as if such invalid portion
thereof had not been incorporated herein.
SECTION 4 Certified copies of the enacting ordinance, the City of Lake Wales
Comprehensive Plan and any amendment thereto, and the Lake Wales
Code of Ordinances shall be located in the Office of the City Clerk of Lake
Wales. The City Clerk shall also make copies available to the public for a
reasonable publication charge.
SECTION 5 This amendment shall not become effective until the state land planning
agency or the Administrative Commission, respectively, issues a final order
determining the amendment is in compliance.

SYNOPSIS: Helmut Wyzisk III, President of Signature H Property Group and authorized
agent for owners, FLOWCC, LLC and VZCREATE, LLC, requests approval of City
Commission for annexation, to amend the Future Land Use Map and Zoning Map, and a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat to allow 558 single-family homes, and 5.195 acres of support
neighborhood commercial on 393+/- acres of land.
Page 2 of 10

RECOMMENDATION
At a regular meeting on October 27, 2026, the Planning and Zoning Board made a
recommendation of approval to City Commission for the following:
A Large-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 146+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of LDR Low Density Residential and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 393+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of R-1C Residential; a
Small-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of NAC Neighborhood Activity Center and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of C-4 Neighborhood
Commercial; and the approval of Oakwood Ridge, a 393+/- acre Preliminary Subdivision
Plat, contingent upon the adoption of Annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City
Commission, and with conditions of approval, finding that the proposed Future Land
Use and Zoning Map designations are compatible with surrounding area, and are
consistent with the City’s Comprehensive Plan and Land Use Regulations, and that the
proposed Preliminary Subdivision Plat is designed to the City’s Land Development
Regulations, and with the following condition:
1.) The applicant shall continue coordination with the existing Oakwood community,
as well as Growth Management Staff, to resolve any outstanding issues related
to tree preservation and school concurrency prior to the City Commission
hearing.
BACKGROUND
The subject 392.53 acres of land is the former Oakwood Golf Club located west of
Capps Road, and north of the Lake Wales Country Club along State Road 60 East.
Approximately 242 acres of the project area are already within the Lake Wales city limits
with a land use designation of Low Density Residential, while the balance of the project
area is within unincorporated Polk County and is petitioned for annexation into the city.
Accompanying the requests for land use and zoning, the applicant has prepared a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat designed to both meet and exceed the standards of the
requested zoning designation of R-1C residential, and includes a small support non-
residential component.
Proposed Development:
  Project Area: 393 acres
  Number of dwelling units: 558
143 65-foot-wide lots
o
287 80-foot-wide lots
o
53 120-foot-wide lots
o
75 alley-loaded townhomes
o
  Three commercial outparcels
  193.4 acres of open space (49.2% of the site)
  27 acres of recreation area provided, including trails and amenities, where 2.11
acres is required by code

  No waivers of strict compliance are requested
LAKE WALES ENVISIONED
Economy E3 Provide a variety of housing to attract employees and industry
Staff Comment: A wide variety of housing options are proposed, including alley-loaded
townhomes, and lots consistent with those within the surrounding area. Through their
community outreach efforts, the applicant received feedback that a need for executive
style housing exists in the city. This resulted in the inclusion of 53 executive lots within
the project.
Economy E4 Concentrate on employee quality of life; promote Lake Wales for remote
workers
Staff Comment: Neighborhoods that include ample trails and recreation opportunities, and
commercial conveniences close to home, may be attractive to remote workers who can
utilize nearby neighborhood amenities without automobile reliance.
Comprehensive Plan: Applicable Objectives and Policies
Policy I.1.2.12 Low Density Residential (LDR) The primary function of the Low Density
Residential (LDR) classification is to provide areas for housing, primarily single-family,
with an overall density of 5 or fewer dwelling units per acre. The LDR classification is
intended to preserve the character of existing single family residential areas with large
lots and to allow the continued development of these areas. It is also intended for
classification of vacant parcels, particularly newly annexed lands on the periphery of the
City.
Location criteria: LDR is appropriate for vacant lands a half mile or more from arterial
roadways and CACs-Community Activity Centers and RACs-Regional Activity Centers.
Existing residential areas with a density of 3 or fewer units per acre on average should
also be assigned the LDR designation.
Typical uses: Housing up to 5 units per acre. Single-family houses. Two- and three-family
units, multi-family units may be allowed depending on compatibility with adjoining
residential areas, the development suitability of the site, and the availability of public
facilities and services. Uses that are customarily found in residential neighborhoods and
compatible with residential development, such as religious, educational, public facility
uses, and assisted living facilities with a maximum FAR of .25. Clustering of housing units
is encouraged to preserve open space and natural resources.
Policy I.1.2.6. Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) The primary function of the
Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) classification is to accommodate businesses and
services oriented to the needs of residents in the surrounding neighborhoods. The NACs
Page 4 of 10

are also intended as village centers in residential developments. They should be designed
as community centers and integral parts of residential neighborhoods, with businesses
located in groups rather than in a strip along a roadway and should be accessible via local
roads and pedestrian/bike paths from nearby residential areas.
Typical uses/intensity: Convenience store, drug store, bakery, florist, professional offices
and personal services. 20,000 - 90,000 SQ.FT. GLA.
Maximum FAR = .25 except in village centers where the maximum FAR shall be 2.0.
Residential uses shall constitute a maximum of 40% of a contiguous NAC district and
shall not exceed 12 units per acre. Location criteria: Accessible to surrounding residential
neighborhoods. Where City water and sanitary sewer are available. Direct access to a
collector or arterial road. A minimum of a mile from another NAC or another activity center
(CAC or RAC). Maximum size 20 acres, except that a mixed-use NAC designed as a
village center in a residential development may encompass up to 50 acres. Development
criteria: • NACs shall be a planned grouping of businesses with a common access point
rather than a strip commercial area; • Access to an NAC shall be from an arterial road or
from a collector road connecting residential areas to a larger roadway. • Pedestrian/bike
paths shall be required to connect the NAC to the surrounding neighborhoods it serves.
• Access to NAC businesses shall be designed primarily for accessibility by the pedestrian
and bicycle rather than by the motorized vehicle. Drive-up banks and restaurants shall
require a special permit. • New village centers shall be designed to mimic traditional
central business districts, with a mix of residential and non-residential uses and
pedestrian oriented layouts.
Objective I.1.5. Desired Urban Growth Pattern A compact, efficient, and sustainable
pattern of development that provides convenient access for residents to urban services
and amenities, avoids urban sprawl, strip development and leap-frog development,
protects natural and historic resources, is compatible with existing land uses and is
coordinated with and consistent with the adopted growth management plans of Polk
County, adjacent municipalities, and with applicable regional and state agency plans and
regulations. Policy I.1.5.1 FLUM - The Future Land Use Map is the key instrument for
achieving Objective I.1.5. The distribution of land use designations on the FLUM shall be
carefully chosen and maintained to promote the desired growth pattern as expressed in
this comprehensive plan, particularly under this objective (Objective I.1.5 – Desired Urban
Growth Pattern) and Objective I.1.2 – Future Land Use Classification System. Locate
future land uses on the Future Land Use Map at densities and intensities that will
discourage urban sprawl and leap-frog development that unduly depletes the physical,
social, and fiscal resources of the city.
Page 5 of 10

Policy I.1.5.2. Functional mix of land uses - The land development regulations shall
promote an integrated mix of residential and non-residential land uses consistent with the
future land use classification system of this element and shall include mechanisms to
promote innovative site design.
Policy I.1.5.5. NACs-Neighborhood Activity Centers shall be designated for convenience
shopping to serve residential areas at a distance from commercial nodes.
Policy I.1.5.12 The land development regulations shall require continued coordination
with Polk County and the Florida Department of Transportation to mitigate the traffic
impacts of development adjacent to Highway 27 and S.R. 60 in the Lake Wales Planning
Area and with agencies as appropriate on access management, facility design options,
right-of-way needs, and on-site traffic flow.
Policy I.1.9.2. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to use, maintain,
development, and improve his or her property for personal use or the use of any other
person, subject to state law and local ordinances.
Policy I.1.9.4. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to dispose of his or
her property through sale or gift.
Zoning: Sec 23-401 District Classifications
R-1C Residential - This district is designed primarily to permit the continued
development of already platted medium density single-family residential areas. The R-
1C zoning designation is intended for assignment to lands classified as MDR - Medium
Density Residential and may be assigned to lands classified as HDR - High Density
Residential on the future land use map of the Comprehensive Plan.
Dimensional and area standards:
Single-family detached:
Minimum lot size: 8,000SF
Minimum street frontage: 50SF
Minimum lot width: 65’
Minimum floor area of home: 1,200SF
Minimum front setback: 0’
Minimum side setback: 10’
Minimum rear setback: 15’
Maximum lot coverage: 60%
Maximum building height: 35’
Single-family detached (townhomes)
Minimum lot size: 2,700SF
Minimum lot width: 25’
Page 6 of 10

Minimum floor area of home: 1,200SF
Minimum front setback: 0’
Minimum exterior side setback: 15’
Minimum interior side setback: 0’
Minimum rear setback: 15’
Maximum lot coverage: 50%
Maximum building height: 35’
Staff Comment: Although the Low-Density Residential land use classification typically
corresponds to the R-1A and R-1B zoning designations, the project proposes a range
of lot sizes, the majority of which exceed the minimum lot dimensions of the R-1A and
R-1B requirements, and the smallest lots exceeding the minimum lot square-footage for
R-1C. Lots within the existing Oakwood neighborhood range from 56’ to 100+’ in width,
while the adjacent Lake Wales Country Club contains a mix of lot sizes and housing
types, such as 100’-wide single-family detached lots, and 50’-wide single-family
attached homes. Additionally, due to the amount of preservation space and open space
proposed, the overall density of the site remains below the low-density threshold, at
1.42 units per gross acre.
C-4 Neighborhood Commercial – This district is designed to permit the development
of local commercial areas to serve surrounding residential areas with small-scale
convenience goods and personal services.
Staff Comment: 5.195 acres of non-residential support uses are proposed within the
northwest area. At such time that these three lots are proposed for development, a
determination will be made by the Administrative Official pursuant to Sec. 23-222.3.b.
determination of major and minor projects, as well as Table 23-421 Permitted Uses, as
to the process for review and approval. The C-4 zoning district permits support
commercial uses such as cultural facilities, daycares, cafes, professional offices, and
personal service.
IMPACT ANALYSIS (provided by applicant per 23-704.2 Burden of proof. The
applicant shall bear the burden of showing compliance with the adopted levels of
service and meeting the concurrency test).
Sec. 23-708 A certificate of concurrency will not be granted for a proposed
development unless the city finds that adequate capacity for concurrency facilities
exists at or above the adopted level of service in order to accommodate the impacts of
the proposed development, or that improvements necessary to bring concurrency
facilities up to their adopted level of service will be in place concurrent with the
impacts of the development.
See Attachment B: Development Impact Analysis

Staff Comments:
1.) 10.3 Projected Demand and Capacity – Statement from the City’s Public Work’s
Director: While capacity may be available, this does not constitute confirmation
or reservation of utility capacity for the proposed development. Final confirmation
will be subject to further review, verification of available system capacity, and the
City's applicable utility requirements.
2.) 13. Trip Generation (Traffic): See Attachment C.
Access to the site is obtained from Burns Avenue (County Road 17A), and
Capps Road, both County Roads. City Planning Staff reached out to the Polk
County Roads and Drainage coordinator on September 9th to give them an
overview of the project and attempt early coordination.
Following the Planning Board Review meeting where the project was discussed
on September 8, the applicant made a requested revision to the plan to include
a future stub out connection to the existing sand mine operation, in anticipation
to the future development of that site once mining ceases. An emergency
services road connection was also created at the southwest portion of the site
to the corner of Sunset Drive and Evergreen Drive.
Conditions of Approval:
1.) A Detailed Landscape Plan, including a tree preservation plan in partnership with
Lake Wales Heritage, is required at Site Development Permit Submittal and will be
reviewed and approved by Staff and the City’s Horticulturist.
2.) Decorative, dark sky friendly street lighting to minimize glare and reduce light
pollution. Street lighting design to be approved by the City, and maintained by the
HOA.
3.) Approval of the PSP does not secure utility capacity or guarantee concurrency.
The developer must enter into a developer’s agreement, capacity queue
agreement and/or utilities agreement with the City, as applicable, and at the
administrative official’s discretion. A utility agreement shall include, but is not
limited to, the transfer of wells if any exist on site.
4.) Although the City does not have front setback requirements, all corner lots shall
have a minimum front setback of 15 feet from the property line within the functional
side yard.
5.) Although access is not needed from an FDOT roadway, the City encourages
coordination with FDOT when future improvements connect to an FDOT Facility
(intersection of Capps Road and State Road 60).
6.) Approval of the map amendments shall be contingent upon the adoption of
annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City Commission.
7.) Approval the PSP shall be contingent upon the adoption of the annexation, future
land use, and zoning ordinances by City Commission.
Page 8 of 10

STAFF FINDINGS:
1. This development site is located within the City’s utility service area and will
maximize existing infrastructure investments by connecting to municipal water and
sewer. According to the City’s Chapter 180 Utility Service Area, the City has a duty
to serve the project with water and sewer.
2. The PSP is designed to meet and/or exceed all requirements of the Land
Development Regulations, including the dimensional and area standards
according to the requested zoning designations, and no waivers of strict
compliance are requested.
3. The recommended Future Land Use categories are generally consistent with the
Future Land Use Element of the Comprehensive Plan.
4. The recommended Zoning Designations are generally consistent with Section 23-
401 of the Land Development Regulations.
5. The neighborhood design is compatible with the existing neighborhoods within the
surrounding area.
6. At a Development Review Committee meeting on August 6, 2026, the Committee
was generally favorable of the proposed development, finding that it was a suitable
redevelopment of the defunct golf course, and that the annexation of the additional
land would improve the form of the city boundary.
7. The applicant has satisfied the Planning and Zoning Board’s additional conditions
of approval by completing the following:
a.) The applicant has partnered with Lake Wales Heritage in order to design an
Olmstead-inspired tree preservation plan (see attachment: Tree Preservation
Statement).
b.) The applicant has conducted meetings with individual property owners within
the existing Oakwood community in order to address specific landscape
buffering and tree preservation opportunities.
c.) The applicant has revised the Development Impact Data to correctly identify
Polk County Public Schools for school concurrency and to better address Level
of Service standards pursuant to Section 23-706 of city code.
d.) The 65-foot-wide lots abutting Country Oaks have been increased in size to 80-
foot-wide lots in order to increase compatibility with the existing home lots.
FISCAL IMPACT
The project may generate $5.2 million in City impact fees.
OTHER OPTIONS
Decline to annex the property.

SECTION 2 If any clause, section or provision of this ordinance or any fee imposed
pursuant to this ordinance shall be declared unconstitutional or invalid for
any reason or cause, the remaining portion of said ordinance or remaining
fees shall be in full force and effect and be valid as if such invalid portion
thereof had not been incorporated herein.
SECTION 3 Certified copies of the enacting ordinance, the City of Lake Wales
Comprehensive Plan and any amendment thereto, and the Lake Wales
Code of Ordinances shall be located in the Office of the City Clerk of Lake
Wales. The City Clerk shall also make copies available to the public for a
reasonable publication charge.
SECTION 4 This amendment shall not become effective until the state land planning
agency or the Administrative Commission, respectively, issues a final order
determining the Future Land Use amendment is in compliance.

and was adopted on Second Reading after Public Hearing on  , 2026.
