SYNOPSIS: Helmut Wyzisk III, President of Signature H Property Group and authorized
agent for owners, FLOWCC, LLC and VZCREATE, LLC, requests approval of City
Commission for annexation, to amend the Future Land Use Map and Zoning Map, and a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat to allow 558 single-family homes, and 5.195 acres of support
neighborhood commercial on 393+/- acres of land.
Page 2 of 10

RECOMMENDATION
At a regular meeting on October 27, 2026, the Planning and Zoning Board made a
recommendation of approval to City Commission for the following:
A Large-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 146+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of LDR Low Density Residential and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 393+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of R-1C Residential; a
Small-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of NAC Neighborhood Activity Center and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of C-4 Neighborhood
Commercial; and the approval of Oakwood Ridge, a 393+/- acre Preliminary Subdivision
Plat, contingent upon the adoption of Annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City
Commission, and with conditions of approval, finding that the proposed Future Land
Use and Zoning Map designations are compatible with surrounding area, and are
consistent with the City’s Comprehensive Plan and Land Use Regulations, and that the
proposed Preliminary Subdivision Plat is designed to the City’s Land Development
Regulations, and with the following condition:
1.) The applicant shall continue coordination with the existing Oakwood community,
as well as Growth Management Staff, to resolve any outstanding issues related
to tree preservation and school concurrency prior to the City Commission
hearing.
BACKGROUND
The subject 392.53 acres of land is the former Oakwood Golf Club located west of
Capps Road, and north of the Lake Wales Country Club along State Road 60 East.
Approximately 242 acres of the project area are already within the Lake Wales city limits
with a land use designation of Low Density Residential, while the balance of the project
area is within unincorporated Polk County and is petitioned for annexation into the city.
Accompanying the requests for land use and zoning, the applicant has prepared a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat designed to both meet and exceed the standards of the
requested zoning designation of R-1C residential, and includes a small support non-
residential component.
Proposed Development:
  Project Area: 393 acres
  Number of dwelling units: 558
143 65-foot-wide lots
o
287 80-foot-wide lots
o
53 120-foot-wide lots
o
75 alley-loaded townhomes
o
  Three commercial outparcels
  193.4 acres of open space (49.2% of the site)
  27 acres of recreation area provided, including trails and amenities, where 2.11
acres is required by code
Page 3 of 10

  No waivers of strict compliance are requested
LAKE WALES ENVISIONED
Economy E3 Provide a variety of housing to attract employees and industry
Staff Comment: A wide variety of housing options are proposed, including alley-loaded
townhomes, and lots consistent with those within the surrounding area. Through their
community outreach efforts, the applicant received feedback that a need for executive
style housing exists in the city. This resulted in the inclusion of 53 executive lots within
the project.
Economy E4 Concentrate on employee quality of life; promote Lake Wales for remote
workers
Staff Comment: Neighborhoods that include ample trails and recreation opportunities, and
commercial conveniences close to home, may be attractive to remote workers who can
utilize nearby neighborhood amenities without automobile reliance.
Comprehensive Plan: Applicable Objectives and Policies
Policy I.1.2.12 Low Density Residential (LDR) The primary function of the Low Density
Residential (LDR) classification is to provide areas for housing, primarily single-family,
with an overall density of 5 or fewer dwelling units per acre. The LDR classification is
intended to preserve the character of existing single family residential areas with large
lots and to allow the continued development of these areas. It is also intended for
classification of vacant parcels, particularly newly annexed lands on the periphery of the
City.
Location criteria: LDR is appropriate for vacant lands a half mile or more from arterial
roadways and CACs-Community Activity Centers and RACs-Regional Activity Centers.
Existing residential areas with a density of 3 or fewer units per acre on average should
also be assigned the LDR designation.
Typical uses: Housing up to 5 units per acre. Single-family houses. Two- and three-family
units, multi-family units may be allowed depending on compatibility with adjoining
residential areas, the development suitability of the site, and the availability of public
facilities and services. Uses that are customarily found in residential neighborhoods and
compatible with residential development, such as religious, educational, public facility
uses, and assisted living facilities with a maximum FAR of .25. Clustering of housing units
is encouraged to preserve open space and natural resources.
Policy I.1.2.6. Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) The primary function of the
Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) classification is to accommodate businesses and
services oriented to the needs of residents in the surrounding neighborhoods. The NACs
Page 4 of 10

are also intended as village centers in residential developments. They should be designed
as community centers and integral parts of residential neighborhoods, with businesses
located in groups rather than in a strip along a roadway and should be accessible via local
roads and pedestrian/bike paths from nearby residential areas.
Typical uses/intensity: Convenience store, drug store, bakery, florist, professional offices
and personal services. 20,000 - 90,000 SQ.FT. GLA.
Maximum FAR = .25 except in village centers where the maximum FAR shall be 2.0.
Residential uses shall constitute a maximum of 40% of a contiguous NAC district and
shall not exceed 12 units per acre. Location criteria: Accessible to surrounding residential
neighborhoods. Where City water and sanitary sewer are available. Direct access to a
collector or arterial road. A minimum of a mile from another NAC or another activity center
(CAC or RAC). Maximum size 20 acres, except that a mixed-use NAC designed as a
village center in a residential development may encompass up to 50 acres. Development
criteria: • NACs shall be a planned grouping of businesses with a common access point
rather than a strip commercial area; • Access to an NAC shall be from an arterial road or
from a collector road connecting residential areas to a larger roadway. • Pedestrian/bike
paths shall be required to connect the NAC to the surrounding neighborhoods it serves.
• Access to NAC businesses shall be designed primarily for accessibility by the pedestrian
and bicycle rather than by the motorized vehicle. Drive-up banks and restaurants shall
require a special permit. • New village centers shall be designed to mimic traditional
central business districts, with a mix of residential and non-residential uses and
pedestrian oriented layouts.
Objective I.1.5. Desired Urban Growth Pattern A compact, efficient, and sustainable
pattern of development that provides convenient access for residents to urban services
and amenities, avoids urban sprawl, strip development and leap-frog development,
protects natural and historic resources, is compatible with existing land uses and is
coordinated with and consistent with the adopted growth management plans of Polk
County, adjacent municipalities, and with applicable regional and state agency plans and
regulations. Policy I.1.5.1 FLUM - The Future Land Use Map is the key instrument for
achieving Objective I.1.5. The distribution of land use designations on the FLUM shall be
carefully chosen and maintained to promote the desired growth pattern as expressed in
this comprehensive plan, particularly under this objective (Objective I.1.5 – Desired Urban
Growth Pattern) and Objective I.1.2 – Future Land Use Classification System. Locate
future land uses on the Future Land Use Map at densities and intensities that will
discourage urban sprawl and leap-frog development that unduly depletes the physical,
social, and fiscal resources of the city.
Page 5 of 10

Policy I.1.5.2. Functional mix of land uses - The land development regulations shall
promote an integrated mix of residential and non-residential land uses consistent with the
future land use classification system of this element and shall include mechanisms to
promote innovative site design.
Policy I.1.5.5. NACs-Neighborhood Activity Centers shall be designated for convenience
shopping to serve residential areas at a distance from commercial nodes.
Policy I.1.5.12 The land development regulations shall require continued coordination
with Polk County and the Florida Department of Transportation to mitigate the traffic
impacts of development adjacent to Highway 27 and S.R. 60 in the Lake Wales Planning
Area and with agencies as appropriate on access management, facility design options,
right-of-way needs, and on-site traffic flow.
Policy I.1.9.2. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to use, maintain,
development, and improve his or her property for personal use or the use of any other
person, subject to state law and local ordinances.
Policy I.1.9.4. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to dispose of his or
her property through sale or gift.
Zoning: Sec 23-401 District Classifications
R-1C Residential - This district is designed primarily to permit the continued
development of already platted medium density single-family residential areas. The R-
1C zoning designation is intended for assignment to lands classified as MDR - Medium
Density Residential and may be assigned to lands classified as HDR - High Density
Residential on the future land use map of the Comprehensive Plan.
Dimensional and area standards:
Single-family detached:
Minimum lot size: 8,000SF
Minimum street frontage: 50SF
Minimum lot width: 65’
Minimum floor area of home: 1,200SF
Minimum front setback: 0’
Minimum side setback: 10’
Minimum rear setback: 15’
Maximum lot coverage: 60%
Maximum building height: 35’
Single-family detached (townhomes)
Minimum lot size: 2,700SF
Minimum lot width: 25’
Page 6 of 10

Minimum floor area of home: 1,200SF
Minimum front setback: 0’
Minimum exterior side setback: 15’
Minimum interior side setback: 0’
Minimum rear setback: 15’
Maximum lot coverage: 50%
Maximum building height: 35’
Staff Comment: Although the Low-Density Residential land use classification typically
corresponds to the R-1A and R-1B zoning designations, the project proposes a range
of lot sizes, the majority of which exceed the minimum lot dimensions of the R-1A and
R-1B requirements, and the smallest lots exceeding the minimum lot square-footage for
R-1C. Lots within the existing Oakwood neighborhood range from 56’ to 100+’ in width,
while the adjacent Lake Wales Country Club contains a mix of lot sizes and housing
types, such as 100’-wide single-family detached lots, and 50’-wide single-family
attached homes. Additionally, due to the amount of preservation space and open space
proposed, the overall density of the site remains below the low-density threshold, at
1.42 units per gross acre.
C-4 Neighborhood Commercial – This district is designed to permit the development
of local commercial areas to serve surrounding residential areas with small-scale
convenience goods and personal services.
Staff Comment: 5.195 acres of non-residential support uses are proposed within the
northwest area. At such time that these three lots are proposed for development, a
determination will be made by the Administrative Official pursuant to Sec. 23-222.3.b.
determination of major and minor projects, as well as Table 23-421 Permitted Uses, as
to the process for review and approval. The C-4 zoning district permits support
commercial uses such as cultural facilities, daycares, cafes, professional offices, and
personal service.
IMPACT ANALYSIS (provided by applicant per 23-704.2 Burden of proof. The
applicant shall bear the burden of showing compliance with the adopted levels of
service and meeting the concurrency test).
Sec. 23-708 A certificate of concurrency will not be granted for a proposed
development unless the city finds that adequate capacity for concurrency facilities
exists at or above the adopted level of service in order to accommodate the impacts of
the proposed development, or that improvements necessary to bring concurrency
facilities up to their adopted level of service will be in place concurrent with the
impacts of the development.
See Attachment B: Development Impact Analysis

Staff Comments:
1.) 10.3 Projected Demand and Capacity – Statement from the City’s Public Work’s
Director: While capacity may be available, this does not constitute confirmation
or reservation of utility capacity for the proposed development. Final confirmation
will be subject to further review, verification of available system capacity, and the
City's applicable utility requirements.
2.) 13. Trip Generation (Traffic): See Attachment C.
Access to the site is obtained from Burns Avenue (County Road 17A), and
Capps Road, both County Roads. City Planning Staff reached out to the Polk
County Roads and Drainage coordinator on September 9th to give them an
overview of the project and attempt early coordination.
Following the Planning Board Review meeting where the project was discussed
on September 8, the applicant made a requested revision to the plan to include
a future stub out connection to the existing sand mine operation, in anticipation
to the future development of that site once mining ceases. An emergency
services road connection was also created at the southwest portion of the site
to the corner of Sunset Drive and Evergreen Drive.
Conditions of Approval:
1.) A Detailed Landscape Plan, including a tree preservation plan in partnership with
Lake Wales Heritage, is required at Site Development Permit Submittal and will be
reviewed and approved by Staff and the City’s Horticulturist.
2.) Decorative, dark sky friendly street lighting to minimize glare and reduce light
pollution. Street lighting design to be approved by the City, and maintained by the
HOA.
3.) Approval of the PSP does not secure utility capacity or guarantee concurrency.
The developer must enter into a developer’s agreement, capacity queue
agreement and/or utilities agreement with the City, as applicable, and at the
administrative official’s discretion. A utility agreement shall include, but is not
limited to, the transfer of wells if any exist on site.
4.) Although the City does not have front setback requirements, all corner lots shall
have a minimum front setback of 15 feet from the property line within the functional
side yard.
5.) Although access is not needed from an FDOT roadway, the City encourages
coordination with FDOT when future improvements connect to an FDOT Facility
(intersection of Capps Road and State Road 60).
6.) Approval of the map amendments shall be contingent upon the adoption of
annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City Commission.
7.) Approval the PSP shall be contingent upon the adoption of the annexation, future
land use, and zoning ordinances by City Commission.
Page 8 of 10

STAFF FINDINGS:
1. This development site is located within the City’s utility service area and will
maximize existing infrastructure investments by connecting to municipal water and
sewer. According to the City’s Chapter 180 Utility Service Area, the City has a duty
to serve the project with water and sewer.
2. The PSP is designed to meet and/or exceed all requirements of the Land
Development Regulations, including the dimensional and area standards
according to the requested zoning designations, and no waivers of strict
compliance are requested.
3. The recommended Future Land Use categories are generally consistent with the
Future Land Use Element of the Comprehensive Plan.
4. The recommended Zoning Designations are generally consistent with Section 23-
401 of the Land Development Regulations.
5. The neighborhood design is compatible with the existing neighborhoods within the
surrounding area.
6. At a Development Review Committee meeting on August 6, 2026, the Committee
was generally favorable of the proposed development, finding that it was a suitable
redevelopment of the defunct golf course, and that the annexation of the additional
land would improve the form of the city boundary.
7. The applicant has satisfied the Planning and Zoning Board’s additional conditions
of approval by completing the following:
a.) The applicant has partnered with Lake Wales Heritage in order to design an
Olmstead-inspired tree preservation plan (see attachment: Tree Preservation
Statement).
b.) The applicant has conducted meetings with individual property owners within
the existing Oakwood community in order to address specific landscape
buffering and tree preservation opportunities.
c.) The applicant has revised the Development Impact Data to correctly identify
Polk County Public Schools for school concurrency and to better address Level
of Service standards pursuant to Section 23-706 of city code.
d.) The 65-foot-wide lots abutting Country Oaks have been increased in size to 80-
foot-wide lots in order to increase compatibility with the existing home lots.
FISCAL IMPACT
The project may generate $5.2 million in City impact fees.
OTHER OPTIONS
Decline to annex the property.

SYNOPSIS: Helmut Wyzisk III, President of Signature H Property Group and authorized
agent for owners, FLOWCC, LLC and VZCREATE, LLC, requests approval of City
Commission for annexation, to amend the Future Land Use Map and Zoning Map, and a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat to allow 558 single-family homes, and 5.195 acres of support
neighborhood commercial on 393+/- acres of land.
Page 2 of 10

RECOMMENDATION
At a regular meeting on October 27, 2026, the Planning and Zoning Board made a
recommendation of approval to City Commission for the following:
A Large-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 146+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of LDR Low Density Residential and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 393+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of R-1C Residential; a
Small-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of NAC Neighborhood Activity Center and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of C-4 Neighborhood
Commercial; and the approval of Oakwood Ridge, a 393+/- acre Preliminary Subdivision
Plat, contingent upon the adoption of Annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City
Commission, and with conditions of approval, finding that the proposed Future Land
Use and Zoning Map designations are compatible with surrounding area, and are
consistent with the City’s Comprehensive Plan and Land Use Regulations, and that the
proposed Preliminary Subdivision Plat is designed to the City’s Land Development
Regulations, and with the following condition:
1.) The applicant shall continue coordination with the existing Oakwood community,
as well as Growth Management Staff, to resolve any outstanding issues related
to tree preservation and school concurrency prior to the City Commission
hearing.
BACKGROUND
The subject 392.53 acres of land is the former Oakwood Golf Club located west of
Capps Road, and north of the Lake Wales Country Club along State Road 60 East.
Approximately 242 acres of the project area are already within the Lake Wales city limits
with a land use designation of Low Density Residential, while the balance of the project
area is within unincorporated Polk County and is petitioned for annexation into the city.
Accompanying the requests for land use and zoning, the applicant has prepared a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat designed to both meet and exceed the standards of the
requested zoning designation of R-1C residential, and includes a small support non-
residential component.

  Number of dwelling units: 558

o

  193.4 acres of open space (49.2% of the site)
  27 acres of recreation area provided, including trails and amenities, where 2.11
acres is required by code

Comprehensive Plan: Applicable Objectives and Policies
Policy I.1.2.12 Low Density Residential (LDR) The primary function of the Low Density
Residential (LDR) classification is to provide areas for housing, primarily single-family,
with an overall density of 5 or fewer dwelling units per acre. The LDR classification is
intended to preserve the character of existing single family residential areas with large
lots and to allow the continued development of these areas. It is also intended for
classification of vacant parcels, particularly newly annexed lands on the periphery of the
City.
Location criteria: LDR is appropriate for vacant lands a half mile or more from arterial
roadways and CACs-Community Activity Centers and RACs-Regional Activity Centers.
Existing residential areas with a density of 3 or fewer units per acre on average should
also be assigned the LDR designation.
Typical uses: Housing up to 5 units per acre. Single-family houses. Two- and three-family
units, multi-family units may be allowed depending on compatibility with adjoining
residential areas, the development suitability of the site, and the availability of public
facilities and services. Uses that are customarily found in residential neighborhoods and
compatible with residential development, such as religious, educational, public facility
uses, and assisted living facilities with a maximum FAR of .25. Clustering of housing units
is encouraged to preserve open space and natural resources.
Policy I.1.2.6. Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) The primary function of the
Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) classification is to accommodate businesses and
services oriented to the needs of residents in the surrounding neighborhoods. The NACs

are also intended as village centers in residential developments. They should be designed
as community centers and integral parts of residential neighborhoods, with businesses
located in groups rather than in a strip along a roadway and should be accessible via local
roads and pedestrian/bike paths from nearby residential areas.
Typical uses/intensity: Convenience store, drug store, bakery, florist, professional offices
and personal services. 20,000 - 90,000 SQ.FT. GLA.
Maximum FAR = .25 except in village centers where the maximum FAR shall be 2.0.
Residential uses shall constitute a maximum of 40% of a contiguous NAC district and
shall not exceed 12 units per acre. Location criteria: Accessible to surrounding residential
neighborhoods. Where City water and sanitary sewer are available. Direct access to a
collector or arterial road. A minimum of a mile from another NAC or another activity center
(CAC or RAC). Maximum size 20 acres, except that a mixed-use NAC designed as a
village center in a residential development may encompass up to 50 acres. Development
criteria: • NACs shall be a planned grouping of businesses with a common access point
rather than a strip commercial area; • Access to an NAC shall be from an arterial road or
from a collector road connecting residential areas to a larger roadway. • Pedestrian/bike
paths shall be required to connect the NAC to the surrounding neighborhoods it serves.
• Access to NAC businesses shall be designed primarily for accessibility by the pedestrian
and bicycle rather than by the motorized vehicle. Drive-up banks and restaurants shall
require a special permit. • New village centers shall be designed to mimic traditional
central business districts, with a mix of residential and non-residential uses and
pedestrian oriented layouts.

pattern of development that provides convenient access for residents to urban services

protects natural and historic resources, is compatible with existing land uses and is
coordinated with and consistent with the adopted growth management plans of Polk

future land use classification system of this element and shall include mechanisms to
promote innovative site design.
Policy I.1.5.5. NACs-Neighborhood Activity Centers shall be designated for convenience
shopping to serve residential areas at a distance from commercial nodes.
Policy I.1.5.12 The land development regulations shall require continued coordination
with Polk County and the Florida Department of Transportation to mitigate the traffic
impacts of development adjacent to Highway 27 and S.R. 60 in the Lake Wales Planning
Area and with agencies as appropriate on access management, facility design options,
right-of-way needs, and on-site traffic flow.
Policy I.1.9.2. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to use, maintain,
development, and improve his or her property for personal use or the use of any other
person, subject to state law and local ordinances.
Policy I.1.9.4. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to dispose of his or
her property through sale or gift.
Zoning: Sec 23-401 District Classifications
R-1C Residential - This district is designed primarily to permit the continued
development of already platted medium density single-family residential areas. The R-
1C zoning designation is intended for assignment to lands classified as MDR - Medium
Density Residential and may be assigned to lands classified as HDR - High Density
Residential on the future land use map of the Comprehensive Plan.
Dimensional and area standards:
Single-family detached:
Minimum lot size: 8,000SF
Minimum street frontage: 50SF
Minimum lot width: 65’
Minimum floor area of home: 1,200SF
Minimum front setback: 0’
Minimum side setback: 10’
Minimum rear setback: 15’
Maximum lot coverage: 60%
Maximum building height: 35’
Single-family detached (townhomes)
Minimum lot size: 2,700SF
Minimum lot width: 25’
Page 6 of 10

Minimum floor area of home: 1,200SF
Minimum front setback: 0’
Minimum exterior side setback: 15’
Minimum interior side setback: 0’
Minimum rear setback: 15’
Maximum lot coverage: 50%
Maximum building height: 35’
Staff Comment: Although the Low-Density Residential land use classification typically
corresponds to the R-1A and R-1B zoning designations, the project proposes a range
of lot sizes, the majority of which exceed the minimum lot dimensions of the R-1A and
R-1B requirements, and the smallest lots exceeding the minimum lot square-footage for
R-1C. Lots within the existing Oakwood neighborhood range from 56’ to 100+’ in width,
while the adjacent Lake Wales Country Club contains a mix of lot sizes and housing
types, such as 100’-wide single-family detached lots, and 50’-wide single-family
attached homes. Additionally, due to the amount of preservation space and open space
proposed, the overall density of the site remains below the low-density threshold, at
1.42 units per gross acre.
C-4 Neighborhood Commercial – This district is designed to permit the development
of local commercial areas to serve surrounding residential areas with small-scale
convenience goods and personal services.
Staff Comment: 5.195 acres of non-residential support uses are proposed within the
northwest area. At such time that these three lots are proposed for development, a
determination will be made by the Administrative Official pursuant to Sec. 23-222.3.b.
determination of major and minor projects, as well as Table 23-421 Permitted Uses, as
to the process for review and approval. The C-4 zoning district permits support
commercial uses such as cultural facilities, daycares, cafes, professional offices, and
personal service.
IMPACT ANALYSIS (provided by applicant per 23-704.2 Burden of proof. The
applicant shall bear the burden of showing compliance with the adopted levels of
service and meeting the concurrency test).
Sec. 23-708 A certificate of concurrency will not be granted for a proposed
development unless the city finds that adequate capacity for concurrency facilities
exists at or above the adopted level of service in order to accommodate the impacts of
the proposed development, or that improvements necessary to bring concurrency
facilities up to their adopted level of service will be in place concurrent with the
impacts of the development.

Staff Comments:
1.) 10.3 Projected Demand and Capacity – Statement from the City’s Public Work’s
Director: While capacity may be available, this does not constitute confirmation
or reservation of utility capacity for the proposed development. Final confirmation
will be subject to further review, verification of available system capacity, and the
City's applicable utility requirements.
2.) 13. Trip Generation (Traffic): See Attachment C.
Access to the site is obtained from Burns Avenue (County Road 17A), and
Capps Road, both County Roads. City Planning Staff reached out to the Polk
County Roads and Drainage coordinator on September 9th to give them an
overview of the project and attempt early coordination.
Following the Planning Board Review meeting where the project was discussed
on September 8, the applicant made a requested revision to the plan to include
a future stub out connection to the existing sand mine operation, in anticipation
to the future development of that site once mining ceases. An emergency
services road connection was also created at the southwest portion of the site
to the corner of Sunset Drive and Evergreen Drive.
Conditions of Approval:
1.) A Detailed Landscape Plan, including a tree preservation plan in partnership with
Lake Wales Heritage, is required at Site Development Permit Submittal and will be
reviewed and approved by Staff and the City’s Horticulturist.
2.) Decorative, dark sky friendly street lighting to minimize glare and reduce light
pollution. Street lighting design to be approved by the City, and maintained by the
HOA.
3.) Approval of the PSP does not secure utility capacity or guarantee concurrency.
The developer must enter into a developer’s agreement, capacity queue
agreement and/or utilities agreement with the City, as applicable, and at the
administrative official’s discretion. A utility agreement shall include, but is not
limited to, the transfer of wells if any exist on site.
4.) Although the City does not have front setback requirements, all corner lots shall
have a minimum front setback of 15 feet from the property line within the functional
side yard.
5.) Although access is not needed from an FDOT roadway, the City encourages
coordination with FDOT when future improvements connect to an FDOT Facility
(intersection of Capps Road and State Road 60).
6.) Approval of the map amendments shall be contingent upon the adoption of
annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City Commission.
7.) Approval the PSP shall be contingent upon the adoption of the annexation, future
land use, and zoning ordinances by City Commission.
Page 8 of 10

STAFF FINDINGS:
1. This development site is located within the City’s utility service area and will
maximize existing infrastructure investments by connecting to municipal water and
sewer. According to the City’s Chapter 180 Utility Service Area, the City has a duty
to serve the project with water and sewer.
2. The PSP is designed to meet and/or exceed all requirements of the Land
Development Regulations, including the dimensional and area standards
according to the requested zoning designations, and no waivers of strict
compliance are requested.
3. The recommended Future Land Use categories are generally consistent with the
Future Land Use Element of the Comprehensive Plan.
4. The recommended Zoning Designations are generally consistent with Section 23-
401 of the Land Development Regulations.
5. The neighborhood design is compatible with the existing neighborhoods within the
surrounding area.
6. At a Development Review Committee meeting on August 6, 2026, the Committee
was generally favorable of the proposed development, finding that it was a suitable
redevelopment of the defunct golf course, and that the annexation of the additional
land would improve the form of the city boundary.
7. The applicant has satisfied the Planning and Zoning Board’s additional conditions
of approval by completing the following:
a.) The applicant has partnered with Lake Wales Heritage in order to design an
Olmstead-inspired tree preservation plan (see attachment: Tree Preservation
Statement).
b.) The applicant has conducted meetings with individual property owners within
the existing Oakwood community in order to address specific landscape
buffering and tree preservation opportunities.
c.) The applicant has revised the Development Impact Data to correctly identify
Polk County Public Schools for school concurrency and to better address Level
of Service standards pursuant to Section 23-706 of city code.
d.) The 65-foot-wide lots abutting Country Oaks have been increased in size to 80-
foot-wide lots in order to increase compatibility with the existing home lots.
FISCAL IMPACT
The project may generate $5.2 million in City impact fees.
OTHER OPTIONS
Decline to annex the property.

SYNOPSIS: Helmut Wyzisk III, President of Signature H Property Group and authorized
agent for owners, FLOWCC, LLC and VZCREATE, LLC, requests approval of City
Commission for annexation, to amend the Future Land Use Map and Zoning Map, and a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat to allow 558 single-family homes, and 5.195 acres of support
neighborhood commercial on 393+/- acres of land.
Page 2 of 10

RECOMMENDATION
At a regular meeting on October 27, 2026, the Planning and Zoning Board made a
recommendation of approval to City Commission for the following:
A Large-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 146+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of LDR Low Density Residential and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 393+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of R-1C Residential; a
Small-Scale Comprehensive Plan Map Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a
Future Land Use designation of NAC Neighborhood Activity Center and a Zoning Map
Amendment to assign 5+/- acres of land a Zoning designation of C-4 Neighborhood
Commercial; and the approval of Oakwood Ridge, a 393+/- acre Preliminary Subdivision
Plat, contingent upon the adoption of Annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City
Commission, and with conditions of approval, finding that the proposed Future Land
Use and Zoning Map designations are compatible with surrounding area, and are
consistent with the City’s Comprehensive Plan and Land Use Regulations, and that the
proposed Preliminary Subdivision Plat is designed to the City’s Land Development
Regulations, and with the following condition:
1.) The applicant shall continue coordination with the existing Oakwood community,
as well as Growth Management Staff, to resolve any outstanding issues related
to tree preservation and school concurrency prior to the City Commission
hearing.
BACKGROUND
The subject 392.53 acres of land is the former Oakwood Golf Club located west of
Capps Road, and north of the Lake Wales Country Club along State Road 60 East.
Approximately 242 acres of the project area are already within the Lake Wales city limits
with a land use designation of Low Density Residential, while the balance of the project
area is within unincorporated Polk County and is petitioned for annexation into the city.
Accompanying the requests for land use and zoning, the applicant has prepared a
Preliminary Subdivision Plat designed to both meet and exceed the standards of the
requested zoning designation of R-1C residential, and includes a small support non-
residential component.
Proposed Development:
  Project Area: 393 acres
  Number of dwelling units: 558
143 65-foot-wide lots
o
287 80-foot-wide lots
o
53 120-foot-wide lots
o
75 alley-loaded townhomes
o
  Three commercial outparcels
  193.4 acres of open space (49.2% of the site)
  27 acres of recreation area provided, including trails and amenities, where 2.11
acres is required by code
Page 3 of 10

  No waivers of strict compliance are requested
LAKE WALES ENVISIONED
Economy E3 Provide a variety of housing to attract employees and industry
Staff Comment: A wide variety of housing options are proposed, including alley-loaded
townhomes, and lots consistent with those within the surrounding area. Through their
community outreach efforts, the applicant received feedback that a need for executive
style housing exists in the city. This resulted in the inclusion of 53 executive lots within
the project.
Economy E4 Concentrate on employee quality of life; promote Lake Wales for remote
workers
Staff Comment: Neighborhoods that include ample trails and recreation opportunities, and
commercial conveniences close to home, may be attractive to remote workers who can
utilize nearby neighborhood amenities without automobile reliance.
Comprehensive Plan: Applicable Objectives and Policies
Policy I.1.2.12 Low Density Residential (LDR) The primary function of the Low Density
Residential (LDR) classification is to provide areas for housing, primarily single-family,
with an overall density of 5 or fewer dwelling units per acre. The LDR classification is
intended to preserve the character of existing single family residential areas with large
lots and to allow the continued development of these areas. It is also intended for
classification of vacant parcels, particularly newly annexed lands on the periphery of the
City.
Location criteria: LDR is appropriate for vacant lands a half mile or more from arterial
roadways and CACs-Community Activity Centers and RACs-Regional Activity Centers.
Existing residential areas with a density of 3 or fewer units per acre on average should
also be assigned the LDR designation.
Typical uses: Housing up to 5 units per acre. Single-family houses. Two- and three-family
units, multi-family units may be allowed depending on compatibility with adjoining
residential areas, the development suitability of the site, and the availability of public
facilities and services. Uses that are customarily found in residential neighborhoods and
compatible with residential development, such as religious, educational, public facility
uses, and assisted living facilities with a maximum FAR of .25. Clustering of housing units
is encouraged to preserve open space and natural resources.
Policy I.1.2.6. Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) The primary function of the
Neighborhood Activity Center (NAC) classification is to accommodate businesses and
services oriented to the needs of residents in the surrounding neighborhoods. The NACs
Page 4 of 10

are also intended as village centers in residential developments. They should be designed
as community centers and integral parts of residential neighborhoods, with businesses
located in groups rather than in a strip along a roadway and should be accessible via local
roads and pedestrian/bike paths from nearby residential areas.
Typical uses/intensity: Convenience store, drug store, bakery, florist, professional offices
and personal services. 20,000 - 90,000 SQ.FT. GLA.
Maximum FAR = .25 except in village centers where the maximum FAR shall be 2.0.
Residential uses shall constitute a maximum of 40% of a contiguous NAC district and
shall not exceed 12 units per acre. Location criteria: Accessible to surrounding residential
neighborhoods. Where City water and sanitary sewer are available. Direct access to a
collector or arterial road. A minimum of a mile from another NAC or another activity center
(CAC or RAC). Maximum size 20 acres, except that a mixed-use NAC designed as a
village center in a residential development may encompass up to 50 acres. Development
criteria: • NACs shall be a planned grouping of businesses with a common access point
rather than a strip commercial area; • Access to an NAC shall be from an arterial road or
from a collector road connecting residential areas to a larger roadway. • Pedestrian/bike
paths shall be required to connect the NAC to the surrounding neighborhoods it serves.
• Access to NAC businesses shall be designed primarily for accessibility by the pedestrian
and bicycle rather than by the motorized vehicle. Drive-up banks and restaurants shall
require a special permit. • New village centers shall be designed to mimic traditional
central business districts, with a mix of residential and non-residential uses and
pedestrian oriented layouts.
Objective I.1.5. Desired Urban Growth Pattern A compact, efficient, and sustainable
pattern of development that provides convenient access for residents to urban services
and amenities, avoids urban sprawl, strip development and leap-frog development,
protects natural and historic resources, is compatible with existing land uses and is
coordinated with and consistent with the adopted growth management plans of Polk
County, adjacent municipalities, and with applicable regional and state agency plans and
regulations. Policy I.1.5.1 FLUM - The Future Land Use Map is the key instrument for
achieving Objective I.1.5. The distribution of land use designations on the FLUM shall be
carefully chosen and maintained to promote the desired growth pattern as expressed in
this comprehensive plan, particularly under this objective (Objective I.1.5 – Desired Urban
Growth Pattern) and Objective I.1.2 – Future Land Use Classification System. Locate
future land uses on the Future Land Use Map at densities and intensities that will
discourage urban sprawl and leap-frog development that unduly depletes the physical,
social, and fiscal resources of the city.
Page 5 of 10

Policy I.1.5.2. Functional mix of land uses - The land development regulations shall
promote an integrated mix of residential and non-residential land uses consistent with the
future land use classification system of this element and shall include mechanisms to
promote innovative site design.
Policy I.1.5.5. NACs-Neighborhood Activity Centers shall be designated for convenience
shopping to serve residential areas at a distance from commercial nodes.
Policy I.1.5.12 The land development regulations shall require continued coordination
with Polk County and the Florida Department of Transportation to mitigate the traffic
impacts of development adjacent to Highway 27 and S.R. 60 in the Lake Wales Planning
Area and with agencies as appropriate on access management, facility design options,
right-of-way needs, and on-site traffic flow.
Policy I.1.9.2. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to use, maintain,
development, and improve his or her property for personal use or the use of any other
person, subject to state law and local ordinances.
Policy I.1.9.4. The City shall understand the right of a property owner to dispose of his or
her property through sale or gift.

services road connection was also created at the southwest portion of the site
to the corner of Sunset Drive and Evergreen Drive.
Conditions of Approval:
1.) A Detailed Landscape Plan, including a tree preservation plan in partnership with
Lake Wales Heritage, is required at Site Development Permit Submittal and will be
reviewed and approved by Staff and the City’s Horticulturist.
2.) Decorative, dark sky friendly street lighting to minimize glare and reduce light
pollution. Street lighting design to be approved by the City, and maintained by the
HOA.
3.) Approval of the PSP does not secure utility capacity or guarantee concurrency.
The developer must enter into a developer’s agreement, capacity queue
agreement and/or utilities agreement with the City, as applicable, and at the
administrative official’s discretion. A utility agreement shall include, but is not
limited to, the transfer of wells if any exist on site.
4.) Although the City does not have front setback requirements, all corner lots shall
have a minimum front setback of 15 feet from the property line within the functional
side yard.
5.) Although access is not needed from an FDOT roadway, the City encourages
coordination with FDOT when future improvements connect to an FDOT Facility
(intersection of Capps Road and State Road 60).
6.) Approval of the map amendments shall be contingent upon the adoption of
annexation Ordinance 2026-32 by City Commission.
7.) Approval the PSP shall be contingent upon the adoption of the annexation, future
land use, and zoning ordinances by City Commission.
Page 8 of 10

STAFF FINDINGS:
1. This development site is located within the City’s utility service area and will
maximize existing infrastructure investments by connecting to municipal water and
sewer. According to the City’s Chapter 180 Utility Service Area, the City has a duty
to serve the project with water and sewer.
2. The PSP is designed to meet and/or exceed all requirements of the Land
Development Regulations, including the dimensional and area standards
according to the requested zoning designations, and no waivers of strict
compliance are requested.
3. The recommended Future Land Use categories are generally consistent with the
Future Land Use Element of the Comprehensive Plan.
4. The recommended Zoning Designations are generally consistent with Section 23-
401 of the Land Development Regulations.
5. The neighborhood design is compatible with the existing neighborhoods within the
surrounding area.
6. At a Development Review Committee meeting on August 6, 2026, the Committee
was generally favorable of the proposed development, finding that it was a suitable
redevelopment of the defunct golf course, and that the annexation of the additional
land would improve the form of the city boundary.
7. The applicant has satisfied the Planning and Zoning Board’s additional conditions
of approval by completing the following:
a.) The applicant has partnered with Lake Wales Heritage in order to design an
Olmstead-inspired tree preservation plan (see attachment: Tree Preservation
Statement).
b.) The applicant has conducted meetings with individual property owners within
the existing Oakwood community in order to address specific landscape
buffering and tree preservation opportunities.
c.) The applicant has revised the Development Impact Data to correctly identify
Polk County Public Schools for school concurrency and to better address Level
of Service standards pursuant to Section 23-706 of city code.
d.) The 65-foot-wide lots abutting Country Oaks have been increased in size to 80-
foot-wide lots in order to increase compatibility with the existing home lots.
FISCAL IMPACT
The project may generate $5.2 million in City impact fees.
OTHER OPTIONS
Decline to annex the property.
Page 9 of 10

RE: Oakwood Ridge, Development Impact Analysis
Dear Ms. Cochella:
Enclosed is the Development Impact Analysis for Oakwood Ridge, a 393.324 acre residential community
proposed within the City of Lake Wales.
The report addresses project description and Comprehensive Plan consistency, proximity to public
improvements, planning assumptions and objectives, existing site conditions, wetlands, floodplain, soils,
utility availability, concurrency and adopted level of service standards, impact fees, public schools, and trip
generation.
Please direct questions on this submittal to me at 305-409-7521 or h3@signatureh.com. Technical questions
may also be directed to Dave Schmitt, P.E., of Dave Schmitt Engineering, Inc.
Sincerely,

2. Section A. Project Description and Comprehensive Plan Consistency
A.1 Location and Legal Description
The project is situated in the City of Lake Wales, Florida, covering a project area of 393.324 acres. Primary
access to the property is provided via CR 17A (Burns Avenue) to the west and Capps Road to the north and
east.
Records from the Polk County Property Appraiser indicate the site encompasses the following parcels within
Section 5 (T30S, R28E) and Section 32 (T29S, R28E), comprising portions of the closed former Oakwood Golf
Club.

Acreages above are as reported by the Polk County Property Appraiser. The project area of 393.324 acres is the surveyed
boundary area shown on the sketch of description and the conceptual site plan, and governs for all calculations in this report.
A.2 Development Program
The development plan provides 558 residential units and 3 commercial out-parcels.
Table 2. Development Program
Product Type	Lot Dimensions	Units	Acres
Single family detached  65 ft. x 130 ft.	143	32.10

I
Total residential units	558	147.20
Commercial out-parcels	3	2.93
Gross residential density is 1.42 dwelling units per acre across the 393.324 acre project area. This is well
below the 5.0 dwelling units per gross acre maximum established for the Low Density Residential
classification, and is consistent with the low-density single-family character of the adjacent neighborhoods
described in Section 5.
The three commercial out-parcels total 2.93 acres with an anticipated 70,000 square feet of gross leasable
area. Out-parcel uses are anticipated to serve the surrounding residential area.
A.3 Land Use, Open Space, and Recreation
The project area of 393.324 acres comprises 16.387 acres of preserved wetland and 376.937 acres of
developable area. The developable area is allocated as follows.
Table 3. Land Use Summary
Component	Acres  Percent of Developable Area

Open space tracts, stormwater management ponds, recreation areas, and preserved wetland together total
193.427 acres, representing 49.2 percent of the 393.324 acre project area. Nearly half of the property is
retained as open space, recreation, conservation, and water management area.
The site plan also includes 3.1 miles of recreational trail. The trail traverses the conservation areas, provides
preserve overlooks, and connects directly to the dog park and the community parks.

Section 23-310 of the Land Development Regulations requires recreation area at a ratio of 1.5 acres per 400
dwelling units. For 558 dwelling units the requirement is 2.09 acres. The plan provides 27.18 acres,
approximately thirteen times the required area.

B.2 Schools
Public education in Polk County is provided by Polk County Public Schools (PCPS), the school district of record
and the authority for school concurrency. School concurrency for this project will be coordinated with Polk
County Public Schools.
The Lake Wales Charter Schools system separately operates several schools within the City of Lake Wales,
including Hillcrest Elementary School, Edward W. Bok Academy North, and Lake Wales High School. Those are
charter schools and are not the basis for the school concurrency determination. They are noted here only
because they are located near the site and are available to residents as schools of choice.

B.4 Summary
Per coordination with City of Lake Wales staff, the facilities listed above will serve the future residents of
Oakwood Ridge. Listed facilities are located between 1.7 and 4.3 miles from the proposed development,
averaging approximately 2.9 miles. Travel times are approximately 5 to 12 minutes depending on traffic
conditions.
4. Section C. Planning Assumptions and Objectives
C.1 Size and Scope of Development
The project area is 393.324 acres. The project includes 558 residential units and 3 commercial out-parcels.
The residential program is set out in Table 2. Gross residential density is 1.42 dwelling units per acre.
C.2 Projected Population
The estimated population at buildout is 1,339 residents, based on 558 units and a ratio of 2.4 persons per
residential unit.
Section 23-706 of the Land Development Regulations provides that population densities shall be calculated at
an average of 2.4 persons per residential unit for all residential development. This factor is also the average
household size reported for Lake Wales city, Florida by the US Census Bureau, American Community Survey
2020-2024 5-Year Estimates (Place FIPS 1238950), which reports 16,426 persons across 6,924 households.
C.3 Phasing and Absorption
The project is anticipated to be constructed in multiple phases and completed by the end of 2036.
5. Existing Conditions
5.1 Existing Conditions of the Property
The project comprises approximately 393.324 acres located east of CR 17A (Burns Avenue) and west of Capps
Road. The site consists of the closed former Oakwood Golf Club and adjoining parcels, and contains maturing
Oakwood Ridge, Development Impact Analysis Page 12 of 24

trees, former fairway and rough areas, wetlands, and surface water features. Several pods of existing
residential development are located between former golf holes and are excluded from the project boundary.
Low-density single-family homes adjoin the site along portions of its boundary. See Exhibit A-1, Aerial
Photograph.
5.2 Existing Conditions of Adjacent Properties
North
The northern boundary is generally bordered by residential development, vacant land, and portions of Burns
Avenue. Existing land uses in this area primarily consist of low-density residential subdivisions and
undeveloped land within the City of Lake Wales jurisdiction. The surrounding residential developments are
composed primarily of single-family homes.
South
Properties located south of the subject site generally consist of portions of the existing Lake Wales Country
Club, open space areas, residential properties, and new single family development. SR 60 is located south of
the overall project area and serves as a major east-west corridor within the vicinity of the development. SR
60 is not directly connected to the project site. Additional surrounding uses include roadway infrastructure,
scattered commercial businesses, and open green space areas.
East
The eastern side of the property is bordered by Capps Road and additional residential development within
the City of Lake Wales. Existing land uses east of the site include low-density residential neighborhoods,
undeveloped land, and scattered agricultural areas.
West
The western boundary of the property is bordered by CR 17A (Burns Avenue) and surrounding residential
communities. Existing uses west of the site primarily include single-family residential developments,
transportation corridors, and undeveloped land. An active sand mine is located west of the property.
6. Topographic Information and Preliminary Drainage Pattern
Based on County LiDAR-derived contour information shown on Exhibit A-2 and the geotechnical investigation
prepared by Terracon Consultants, Inc. (Geotechnical Engineering Report, Oakwood Site, Terracon Project
No. H1235312), the project site generally contains gentle relief with elevations ranging from approximately
+95 feet to +130 feet NAVD88. Most of the site ranges between approximately +105 feet and +115 feet
NAVD88, while low-lying areas are generally located between elevations +95 feet and +100 feet.
Drainage patterns generally flow toward existing low areas, wetlands, and surface water features located
throughout the site. Existing water features have measured water levels of approximately +97 feet to +100
feet in the northern portion of the site and up to +105 feet in the southern portion. Stormwater management
for the proposed development consists of 25 stormwater ponds distributed throughout the project, totaling
44.400 acres. See Exhibit A-2, Contour Map.
Stormwater management will be designed in accordance with applicable Southwest Florida Water
Management District (SWFWMD) criteria. Off-line retention systems will be required to treat the first inch of
Oakwood Ridge, Development Impact Analysis Page 13 of 24

runoff, or the first 0.5 inch for sub-basins less than 100 acres. Treatment volume must be recovered within 72
hours to maintain system functionality and meet water quality requirements.
7. Wetlands
A field wetland delineation of the property was performed by Stillwater Environmental. Seven distinct
freshwater wetland areas are identified within the property boundary, designated Wetland No. 1 through
Wetland No. 7, totaling 18.557 acres. Of that total, 16.387 acres are preserved and 2.170 acres are proposed
for impact. Delineated wetland limits and flag locations are shown on Exhibit A-5, Wetlands Map.

Proposed wetland impacts total 2.170 acres, or 11.7 percent of the delineated wetland area, and are limited
to Wetlands 1, 4, and 6. Impacts will be permitted through the Southwest Florida Water Management District
and the US Army Corps of Engineers, with mitigation provided in accordance with agency requirements.
The National Wetlands Inventory (NWI) classifies these systems primarily as Freshwater Ponds (Palustrine)
and Freshwater Emergent Wetlands, consistent with the delineated conditions. Exhibit A-5 also identifies the
permitted stormwater ponds located on the property under existing district permits.
Preserved wetlands are excluded from the developable area and are integrated into the community design as
central conservation tracts within the 105.460 acres of open space tracts, as summarized in Table 3.
8. FEMA Flood Map
The FEMA Flood Insurance Rate Map (FIRM) panel 12105C0565H was most recently revised on December 22,
2016. According to the FIRM, portions of the subject property are located within Zone A.
Zone A is a Special Flood Hazard Area (SFHA), also known as the 100-year floodplain. Areas within Zone A
have a one percent annual chance of flooding.
Multiple Zone A areas are located on the property, within the following parcels:

11.1 Sanitary Sewer
Applying the adopted level of service standard of 100 gallons per capita per day to a projected population of
1,339 residents yields a residential average daily wastewater flow of 133,920 gallons per day. Adding the
estimated commercial out-parcel flow of 7,000 gallons per day gives a total average daily flow of 140,920
gallons per day. Maximum daily flow, calculated as average daily flow plus 13 percent in accordance with the
adopted standard, is 159,240 gallons per day. The development will connect to the existing municipal
collection system as described in Section 10.2.
11.2 Potable Water
Applying the adopted level of service standard of 122 gallons per capita per day to a projected population of
1,339 residents yields a residential average daily potable water demand of 163,382 gallons per day. Adding
the estimated commercial out-parcel demand of 8,540 gallons per day gives a total average daily demand of
171,922 gallons per day. The development will connect to the existing municipal potable water system as
described in Section 10.2.
Oakwood Ridge, Development Impact Analysis Page 18 of 24

11.3 Solid Waste
The City of Lake Wales Comprehensive Plan establishes a level of service standard of 7.3 pounds per capita
per day for solid waste collection. Applying that standard to a projected population of 1,339 residents yields a
generation rate of 9,776 pounds per day, or approximately 4.89 tons per day and 1,784 tons per year.
Non-hazardous solid waste generated by the development will be collected for disposal at a designated Polk
County landfill or recycled, consistent with the Comprehensive Plan and with the 75 percent recycling rate
established under Chapter 403, Florida Statutes. Residential collection will be provided through the City of
Lake Wales solid waste program at the curbside and recycling service levels set out in Appendix B.
Certification from Polk County for solid waste concurrency will be obtained in accordance with Section 23-
706.
11.4 Roads
A Traffic Impact Analysis methodology has been prepared by Traffic Planning and Design, Inc. and submitted
to the Polk Transportation Planning Organization and the City of Lake Wales. The methodology establishes
the study area through a significance test against the Polk County threshold of five percent of adopted
capacity, and the Traffic Impact Analysis will demonstrate compliance with the level of service standards
established in the Transportation Element. Certification from Polk County for roadway concurrency will be
provided. Trip generation is summarized in Section 14.
11.5 Drainage
Stormwater management for the project will be designed in accordance with Southwest Florida Water
Management District criteria as described in Section 6, and will meet the level of service standards
established in the Drainage Element of the Comprehensive Plan. Issuance of a permit from the Southwest
Florida Water Management District for drainage facilities constitutes evidence of compliance under Section
23-706. The plan provides 44.400 acres of stormwater management ponds across 25 pond areas.
11.6 Recreation and Open Space
Applying the adopted community park level of service standard of 2 acres per 1,000 population to a
projected population of 1,339 residents yields a requirement of 2.68 acres. The plan provides 27.18 acres of
recreation area in seven tracts, as set out in Table 5, within a total of 193.427 acres of open space, recreation,
stormwater, and preserved wetland area. The project exceeds the adopted standard by a substantial margin
and provides all recreation area on site.
11.7 Public Schools
The demand for public school capacity is based upon the number of units proposed and the student
generation ratios approved for use by the Polk County School Board, as set out in Section 13. A school
concurrency determination will be obtained from Polk County Public Schools.
12. Impact Fees
Impact fees are charges assessed on new development to help pay for public infrastructure required to
accommodate the new development. Impact fees are generally charged at the issuance of a project building
permit. The project is subject to both City of Lake Wales and Polk County impact fees.

Executive Summary
Based on coordination with the City of Lake Wales, FDOT D1 Systems Planning was requested for assistance in
providing a preliminary analysis of the impacts of the proposed Oakwood Ridge development to the intersection of SR
60 and Capps Road. Oakwood Ridge is proposed as a primarily residential development with commercial outparcels.
There is no anticipated buildout date at this time.The following memorandum summarizes the anticipated impacts to
the SR 60 at Capps Road intersection, from a high-level planning perspective.
As a disclaimer, the following analysis is based on estimates and assumptions from the limited available
data. As the development application is formalized, the applicant will submit a full TIA with turning movement
count data and a model distribution, so the results may differ from the following. Ultimately, the outcomes of
this analysis should be taken as high-level suggestions rather than requirements.
The following analysis suggests that the intersection of SR 60 at Capps Road may require the following improvements
to serve project traffic, subject to the final development plan, data collection, design feasibility, and permitting
procedures for local and state agencies:
 Signalization, based on Warrants 1A and 1B of the Manual on Uniform Traffic Control Devices (MUTCD),
Chapter 4C
 Extensions of the eastbound left-turn lane and westbound right-turn lane on SR 60, based on Florida Design
Manual (FDM), Exhibit 212-1
 Addition of a southbound left-turn lane and/or a southbound right-turn lane on Capps Road, based on the
2023 FDOT Multimodal Access Management Guidebook, Section 6

Trip Distribution and Assignment
The trip distribution was manually estimated based on engineering judgement and model-based assignments from
TIAs for nearby developments in Lake Wales. The development is anticipated to have two main access points (the
types of access are not confirmed at this time):
 Access on Capps Road, north of SR 60
 Access on CR 17A/Masterpiece Road, east of CR 17B/Buckmoore Road
SR 60 and CR 17A serve the main population center of Lake Wales and also connect to the major north-south
principal arterial of US 27 to the west. Therefore, the majority of project traffic distributed to/from the west. SR 60 east
of Capps Road serves as a regional east-west arterial terminating in Vero Beach, while CR 17A serves as a less
direct alternative north-south corridor to SR 17. Therefore, a minority of traffic was distributed to/from the east/north
along these routes.
The trip distribution was summarized as follows:
 50% to/from the west on SR 60
 15% to/from the east on SR 60
 30% to/from the west on CR 17A/Masterpiece Rd
 5% to/from the north/east on CR 17A/Masterpiece Rd
At SR 60 and Capps Road, the trip distribution was applied to the external entering and exiting trips for the AM and
PM peak hours to estimate the trip assignment. The trip assignment is summarized as follows:
 Entering Trips:
o Eastbound left-turn from SR 60 to Capps Road (50%): 136 AM trips and 193 PM trips
o Westbound right-turn from SR 60 to Capps Road (15%): 41 AM trips and 58 PM trips
 Exiting Trips:
o Southbound right-turn from Capps Road to SR 60 (50%): 216 AM trips and 137 PM trips
o Southbound left-turn from Capps Road to SR 60 (15%): 65 AM trips and 42 PM trips
Warrant for Signalization
Currently, the intersection of SR 60 and Capps Rd operates as a three-leg, unsignalized full median opening with stop
control on the southbound approach. The Manual on Uniform Traffic Control Devices stipulates nine (9) warrants to
justify the need for a traffic signal. As part of this preliminary analysis, warrant 1 was considered as the main
justification based on reasonable estimates of anticipated project and background volumes on SR 60.
For warrant 1, the MUTCD compares hourly volumes for conflicting movements. For this analysis, the movements
considered were the eastbound left-turn from SR 60 to Capps Road and the westbound through volumes on SR 60.

 Note that the following analysis does not consider background left-turns (non-project traffic), therefore, is a
best-case scenario that underestimates actual traffic.
Based on the estimated volumes, the development traffic is likely to justify a signal based on meeting warrants 1A and
1B at the 70% threshold. The MUTCD warrant threshold tables and ITE time-of-day distributions are provided in
Attachment B.

Page 5	Memorandum
Turn Lane Extensions and Warrants
Turn lane requirements were evaluated based on guidance from the latest 2025 Florida Design Manual, Exhibit 212-1
and the 2023 Multimodal FDOT Access Management Guidebook, Section 6. The following improvements are
preliminary suggestions based on rule-of-thumb assumptions, and would be subject to operational review and may be
limited by unknown design feasibility constraints. The turn lane warrants and deceleration length guidelines are
provided in Attachment C.
State Road 60
There currently exist one eastbound left-turn lane and one westbound right-turn lane at this intersection on SR 60. SR
60 in this area currently has a posted speed limit of 65 mph. Based on the posted speed limit of 65 mph and the latest
2025 FDM 212-1, the turn lane length should be calculated as the 95th percentile queue plus a minimum deceleration
distance of 460 ft.
An operational analysis would be required to estimate the 95th percentile queue, however, such a design-level
analysis cannot be conducted without turning movement counts. However, it is likely that such an analysis would
determine that turn lane extensions would be required to serve the project traffic impact.
Capps Road
There currently exist no turn lanes on the southbound approach of Capps Road at SR 60. Capps Road on this
approach currently has a posted speed limit of 45 mph.
Based on the posted speed limit of 45 mph and Figure 78 of the latest 2023 FDOT Access Management Guidebook, a
left-turn lane would be warranted. Similarly, based on Figure 74 of the guidebook, a right-turn lane would likely be
warranted if the intersection were to remain as stop-control, depending on background (non-project) volume counts.
However, based on the assumption that the intersection would warrant signalization, the consideration for a right-turn
lane would need to account for other factors including operational needs, operating speeds, crash history, truck
volumes, and others.

OAKWOOD RIDGE · CITY OF LAKE WALES
Tree	Preservation	and	Landscape	Commitment
Applicant statement for inclusion in the staff report
OUR COMMITMENT
Oakwood Ridge is committed to preserving significant oaks and stands of oaks wherever
possible, with particular emphasis on environmentally sensitive areas and the perimeter
buffers adjoining existing neighborhoods. We view the mature tree canopy on this property as
one of its defining assets, and we intend for it to remain a defining feature of the community
once it is built.
PRESERVATION ALREADY BUILT INTO THE PLAN
Tree preservation has shaped this plan from the start, not as an afterthought. Informed by a
tree survey of the property, we removed entire sections of development from earlier versions
of the plan to avoid disturbing heavily forested areas. The current Preliminary Site Plan is
designed specifically to keep natural areas, conservation lands, and the most heavily wooded
portions of the site intact.
PROTECTING THE TREES OUR NEIGHBORS KNOW
We have identified specific areas on the plan, particularly along the edges where existing
residents live, where existing trees will be maintained. These trees matter for more than their
size. They serve as a natural buffer between existing homes and new development, and they
are trees that residents have lived alongside and grown to love for years. Their removal is not
necessary for this project to succeed, and keeping them is part of what makes this
development mutually beneficial for existing residents, future residents, and the City.
A COLLABORATIVE, OLMSTED-INSPIRED APPROACH
We have invited Lake Wales Heritage to work alongside us in developing a Landscape and
Tree Preservation Plan for Oakwood Ridge. Lake Wales has a direct connection to the Olmsted
legacy through the landscape of Bok Tower Gardens, and our intention is to apply Olmsted-

inspired design principles to the future landscape plans for this community: working with the
natural topography, framing long views, preserving and celebrating existing tree canopy, and
creating a connected landscape that feels like it belongs to Lake Wales.
TREE REPLACEMENT
Where tree removal is unavoidable, Oakwood Ridge will replace trees at or above the levels
required by the City of Lake Wales Land Development Regulations.
NEXT STEPS
• Continue working with Lake Wales Heritage to define the Olmsted-inspired design
approach, preservation priorities, and tree selection.
• Prepare a Landscape and Tree Preservation Plan that identifies preserved trees and stands,
perimeter buffer treatments, and replacement plantings.
• Submit that plan to the City as part of subsequent development review.
Helmut Wyzisk III, President
Signature H Property Group
305-409-7521 · h3@signatureh.com
